Title: Changed symbol in docs table

Description:
## What does this MR do?

[Community feedback on the Feature flags page](https://docs.gitlab.com/runner/configuration/feature-flags.html#comment-5621982880) indicating that the `x` in the Deprecated column is confusing. It is unclear if it means `yes, deprecated` or `no, not deprecated`.

This MR updates the related files to use `**{dotted-circle}** No` or `**{check-circle}** Yes` based on guidance in [Documentation Style Guide](https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/development/documentation/styleguide/#feature-tables).

=================== PR 7533   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/7533
--- [title_description] 0-4004
Title: Docker+machine: Add RemoveWithoutStop to skip docker-machine stop before rm

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Adds `RemoveWithoutStop` to `[runners.machine]`. When `true`, the provider removes a machine with `docker-machine rm -y` directly instead of running `docker-machine stop` first. Default `false` keeps the current stop-then-remove sequence.

`rm -f` is not used in this path: it drops the local store entry even when the GCE delete failed, which would hide failures from the retry loop. It stays reserved for the give-up path.

## Why was this MR needed?

The SaaS shards run `MaxBuilds = 1`, so every job is one VM lifecycle, and during [INC-14929](https://app.incident.io/gitlab/incidents/14929) the runner managers hit the GCE "Write requests per minute per region" quota. That is the simplified `writes_per_region` metric, which charges per method rather than per request ([quota metrics reference](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/api/compute-api-quota-metrics)): `bulkInsert` is 6 units, `stop`, `delete` and `setLabels` are 1, `*Operations.wait` is 2 on the read side. GCE API calls observed on one production project, per minute and per VM:

| method | /min | per VM | quota | cost | units/min |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|
| `RegionInstances.BulkInsert` | 420 | 1.33 | write | 6 | 2,520 |
| `Instances.Stop` | 317 | 1.0 | write | 1 | 317 |
| `Instances.Delete` | 317 | 1.0 | write | 1 | 317 |
| `Instances.SetLabels` (heartbeat) | 27 | 0.08 | write | 1 | 27 |
| `Instances.Get` | 1,617 | 5.1 | read | 1 | 1,617 |
| `ZoneOperations.Wait` | 661 | 2.1 | read | 2 | 1,322 |
| `RegionOperations.Wait` | 425 | 1.3 | read | 2 | 850 |
| `Projects.Get` | 320 | 1.0 | global read | 1 | 320 |
| `Disks.Get` (all 404) | 319 | 1.0 | read | 1 | 319 |

`Instances.Stop` is 1 of  [...]
--- [commits] 4005-5273
Commit messages:
docker+machine: Add FastRemove to skip docker-machine stop before rm

On GCE the stop step is Instances.Stop plus a wait for the guest to
finish its ACPI shutdown, median ~20s on the SaaS shards, before rm
issues Instances.Delete (~7s). Delete also sends ACPI shutdown to a
running instance, so the separate stop only adds a serial operation
and a wait. Removing machines count toward the runner limit, so that
time is capacity held in machineStateRemoving.

FastRemove = true goes straight to rm -y. Default is false, which keeps
the current stop-then-remove sequence. rm -f is not used in this path
because it drops the local store entry even when the GCE delete failed,
which would hide failures from the retry loop.

docker+machine: Match create() signature in FastRemove test after rebase

docker+machine: Rename FastRemove to RemoveWithoutStop

Removal is not faster in any way that matters: GCE takes ~15s to mark
an instance TERMINATED after the guest halts, in both the stop and the
delete path. What the option does is drop the Instances.Stop write
request per VM.

docker+machine: Drop the comment on removeWithoutStop

docker+machine: gofmt details.go

Apply 2 suggestion(s) to 2 file(s)

Co-authored-by: Isaac Durham <idurham@gitlab.com>

=================== PR 2606   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2606
--- [title_description] 0-1030
Title: Display feature flags that are set to a non-default status

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Displays a list of feature flags and their status (on, off) at the beginning of the job.

![image](/uploads/6a4a473f4b7d45870b5903bf7343d304/image.png)

Only feature flags that have a non-default status set are shown to avoid how verbose this list could otherwise get. The runner version can always be used to determine which feature flags were on otherwise.

## Why was this MR needed?

Feature flags can be set in a variety of ways, but it's sometimes difficult to know whether you've done it correctly.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Unit test:
```sh
go test -run TestBuild_getFeatureFlagInfo ./common
```

Manual test:
```yaml
image: busybox:latest
variables:
  FF_USE_FASTZIP: "true"

showvars:
  stage: test
  script:
    - echo "hello"
```

Should print an introduction similar to the image above.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/27288

Comment by @sxuereb:
**issue:** I see that you already [discussed testing](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2606#note_483577766), but I think the drawbacks of having untested code is worse then having a little bit of coupling with feature flags, I appreciate that both have trade-offs. If we are worried that this test will create a lot of churn we can use `FF_CMD_DISABLE_DELAYED_ERROR_LEVEL_EXPANSION` which is not deprecated and probably won't be touched ever since `cmd` is on a feature freeze for customers.

If we have a test like below we do have some coupling yes, but I think it might be worth having the test for it

<details>
<summary> TestBuild_getFeatureFlagInfo </summary>

```go
func TestBuild_getFeatureFlagInfo(t *testing.T) {
	const changedFeatureFlags = "FF_CMD_DISABLE_DELAYED_ERROR_LEVEL_EXPANSION:true"
	tests := []struct {
		value          string
		expectedStatus string
	}{
		{
			value:          "true",
			expectedStatus: changedFeatureFlags,
		},
		{
			value:          "1",
			expectedStatus: changedFeatureFlags,
		},
		{
			value:          "invalid",
			expectedStatus: "",
		},
	}

	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.value, func(t *testing.T) {
			b := Build{
				JobResponse: JobResponse{
					Variables: JobVariables{
						{
							Key:    featureflags.CmdDisableDelayedErrorLevelExpansion,
							Value:  tt.value,
							Public: true,
						},
					},
				},
			}

			assert.Equal(t, tt.expectedStatus, b.getFeatureFlagInfo())
		})
	}
}
```

</details>

On a bigger note, maybe this kind of coupling is a code smell. Maybe as a future refactor we should have these feature flags part of the `Build` struct so we can override in tests. Like this we get rid of the "global" variable that we have as a feature flag.

=================== PR 2744   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2744
--- [title_description] 0-1000
Title: Add supported failure reasons for build errors

Description:
## What does this MR do?

The failure reasons for a build error are likely to change over time. However, these failure reasons also need to be supported by the GitLab instance they're sent to.

Because Runner and GitLab versions can be mismatched, a common set of failure reasons need to be established.

This MR implements filtering for supported failure reasons. It combines widely accepted failure reasons (reasons we know all GitLab versions support) with the supported version GitLab advertises. If the failure reason is not in this list, it falls back to the failure reason `UnknownFailure`.

## Why was this MR needed?

We've been accidentally sending GitLab unsupported failure reasons.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Run the test `TestBuild_setTraceStatus`. This test checks the behaviour for when GitLab does, or does not, send a list of failure reasons.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #27449

--- [commits] 1001-1065
Commit messages:
Add supported failure reasons for build errors

--- [review_thread] 5080-5267

@ajwalker: Done! :thumbsup:

--- [review_thread] 5268-5449

@ayufan: There's always `unknown_failure:`. Could we use that instead?

--- [review_thread] 5450-6295
Review thread on common/build.go:
@pedropombeiro: **suggestion**: @ajwalker seems like this could be easily split to a separate function, which we could then call:

```go
		supported := supportsFailureReason(failureReason)
```

```go
func (b *Build) supportsFailureReason(failureReason JobFailureReason) bool {
	// GitLab provides a list of supported failure reasons with the job. Should the list be empty, we use
	// the minmum subset of failure reasons we know all GitLab instances support.
	supported := false
	for _, reason := range append(
		b.Features.FailureReasons,
		ScriptFailure,
		RunnerSystemFailure,
		JobExecutionTimeout,
	) {
		supported = reason == failureReason
		if supported {
			break
		}
	}

	// if unsupported reason, default to UnknownFailure
	if !supported {
		failureReason = UnknownFailure
	}

	return supported
}
```

Comment by @ajwalker:
I originally intended on adding this to the gitlab client, as that seems like the most logical place.

Unfortunetly, it doesn't appear to be simpler, due to the loose coupling I mentioned. The GitLab client doesn't seem to have access to the `Features`.

If we don't like what I've done here, there's a couple of options, but the changes required grow.

- We could update the `UpdateJob` interface to accept `GitLabFeatures`
   ```diff
   -func (n *GitLabClient) UpdateJob(config common.RunnerConfig, jobCredentials *common.JobCredentials, jobInfo common.UpdateJobInfo) common.UpdateJobResult {
   +func (n *GitLabClient) UpdateJob(config common.RunnerConfig, features common.GitLabFeatures, jobCredentials *common.JobCredentials, jobInfo common.UpdateJobInfo) common.UpdateJobResult {
   ```
- We could update `UpdateJobInfo` to contain `GitLabFeatures` (but that doesn't really feel like it _should_ contain it).
- etc.

@ayufan Thoughts?

=================== PR 2379   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2379
--- [title_description] 0-1520
Title: Fix Windows runner helper docker container

Description:
## What does this MR do?

- Removes a recursive default include path from the system git config.
- Removes the use of `Start-Process`. This was presumably used to create a shell with the newly set environment variables. However, it appears doing this has swallowed the `git config` error.

## Why was this MR needed?

The git version we've upgraded to, `2.27.0`, contains an include that is recursive.

`MinGit`, the install of git we use, is not recommended to be installed to `Program Files\git` (https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/issues/2387#issuecomment-549787688). 

The recommended fix is to either change installation location or unset the included paths. Eventually, we may want to consider changing path.

## What's the best way to test this MR?
```
docker build -t win -f .\dockerfiles\runner-helper\Dockerfile.x86_64_servercore .\dockerfiles\runner-helper\ --build-arg BASE_IMAGE_TAG=mcr.microsoft.com/windows/servercore:1909-amd64 --build-arg GIT_VERSION=2.27.0 --build-arg GIT_VERSION_BUILD=1 --build-arg GIT_LFS_VERSION=2.11.0  --build-arg GIT_256_CHECKSUM=bdb8c1560eca39f4d99b07705006c00d94a3f8612501046a0f89353afc5307fa --build-arg GIT_LFS_256_CHECKSUM=ca05b0b9bd39d99665045776a5d0d5c7bfa2605a6118c2f489bfd40c2c3e4f2c

docker run -it win:latest powershell.exe

# no error running git config means we're good
git config user.name
```

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/239013

--- [review_thread] 3822-4677
Review thread on dockerfiles/runner-helper/Dockerfile.x86_64_servercore:
@sxuereb: **question:** What is this preventing Git to do? Update some system settings? Can this be a breaking change for some user?
@ajwalker: @steveazz These two environment variables we're setting should just be temporary (so there shouldn't be any breaking change).

The `PATH` is manually updated so that we don't need to launch a new shell to observe the system environment updates on the previous line.

The `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` is set because using `git config --system --no-includes` isn't enough to prevent Git from still trying to include the `system` git config _even though_ it's the config we're trying to specifically edit. This tells it to skip including the system git config, so we don't run into the recursion problem whilst trying to fix the recursion problem.

=================== PR 3881   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3881
--- [title_description] 0-1389
Title: Generate random system_id for run-single mode

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Generate a new random `system_id` for every `gitlab-runner run-single` invocation, preventing a `<nil>` dereference panic.

### Alternative approaches

* Leave the `system_id` field blank, null or unspecified: discarded because it would require more code changes and might go against the purpose of https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29390.
* Try to load an existing `system_id` from the file system: discarded because the `system_id` storage approach of https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3758 uses paths [relative to `config.toml`](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/4be0cfb3ae433e1ca8ca394695f25df880940432/commands/config.go#L66) but this file is irrelevant for the `gitlab-runner run-single` command.

## Why was this MR needed?

Closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29592 and fixes the `gitlab-runner run-single` command.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

See https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29592 for a minimal example, albeit it's a bit worrying to realize that automated testing doesn't cover this functionality.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29592 & https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29390

--- [commits] 1390-1516
Commit messages:
Generate random SystemIDState for run-single mode

Closes #29592

Use logrus.Fatal instead of logrus.Error

--- [review_thread] 1986-2335
Review thread on commands/single.go:
@pedropombeiro: **suggestion**:  @0x2b3bfa0 should this be a fatal error instead? If we don't prevent the program from continuing, we'll see the same panic ahead.

```suggestion:-0+0
		logrus.WithError(err).Fatal("Failed to generate random system ID")
```
@0x2b3bfa0: Yes, thank you very much! :face_palm_tone1:

Thanks for the ping, Axel! And thank you for creating an issue and a merge request to address this @0x2b3bfa0!

As you found out, there seems to be insufficient test coverage around `RunSingleCommand`, which allowed this bug to go under the radar. We'll need to see if we can at least add some integration tests around it. Your analysis and fix seems correct to me. If the runner command is executed on a host with a stable system ID, then we should get the same system ID every time. The exception would be running `run-single` inside a Docker container that doesn't report a stable system ID, and at that point we'd generate a new random ID (which isn't saved at the moment) on each run.

=================== PR 535   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/535
--- [title_description] 0-929
Title: Fix regression introduced in the way how `exec` parses `.gitlab-ci.yml`

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Fixes regression introduced in v9.0.0 while refactorizing `exec` command.

For current master branch (and planned 9.3.0 release) the fix is added as part of !596.

## Why was this MR needed?

If some setting of a job can be configured at global level and at job level in `.gitlab-ci.yml` file, then job's definition should have a priority over the general definition. While refactorizing `exec` it was by mistake written in the wrong order.

## Are there points in the code the reviewer needs to double check?

=================== PR 6056   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/6056
--- [title_description] 0-1793
Title: Upgrade step-runner to version 0.24.0

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Upgrades the step-runner from version 0.22.0 to 0.24.0.

## Step runner changes

#### v0.24.0

- Job variables can be found using `${{ vars.VAR_NAME }}` instead of `${{ job.VAR_NAME }}`. See [345](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/345).
- Fix issue where functions cannot be fetched locally using an expression. See [350](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/350).
- The function directory can be found using `${{ func_dir }}` instead of `${{ step_dir }}`. See [346](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/346).
- Produce and sign a step-runner binaries checksum file. See [348](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/348).

#### v0.23.0

- Expressions are evaluated using a new expression language, [Moa](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/moa). See [337](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/337).
- Remove warning about the legacy image. See [338](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/338).
- Reference Functions using `func:`, referencing `step:` as a fallback. See [340](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/340).
- Functions expect `func.yml`, and load `step.yml` as a fallback. See [339](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/339).
- Link to step-runner binaries in the release notes. See [344](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/344).

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

- https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/issues/309+
- https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/issues/339+
- https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/582907+
- https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/issues/304+

--- [conversation] 4964-5744
Comment by @timofurrer:
@cam_swords LGTM :handshake: 

One off topic question though:

> * Job variables can be found using `${{ vars.VAR_NAME }}` instead of `${{ job.VAR_NAME }}`. See [345](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/345).

For the CI Job Inputs we use the `job.inputs` map to hold all inputs, so inputs become accessible with e.g. `${{ job.inputs.some_input_name }}`. Does it make sense to improve the alignment here with the `step-runner`? Eventually, we might want to access variables in moa in the runner, too - a natural fit might be `${{ job.vars.VAR_NAME }}`. 

What's the reason in the `step-runner` to access variables as `${{ job.VAR_NAME }}` instead of e.g. `${{ job.vars.VAR_NAME }}` ? Do you even see a necessity to align? :thinking:

--- [conversation] 5745-6450
Comment by @cam_swords:
> What's the reason in the `step-runner` to access variables as `${{ job.VAR_NAME }}` instead of e.g. `${{ job.vars.VAR_NAME }}`

@timofurrer, do you mean "to access variables as `${{ vars.VAR_NAME }}`"?

Job variables are a mashup of variables set by different places ([example](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/1809#note_225636231)). The point of using `vars` instead of `job.vars` is that it gives us the option of exposing explicit access to the job variables later on. For example, `vars.XYZ` could be the mashup of variables, while `group[xyz].vars.XYZ` and `job.vars.XYZ` could return only what was explicitly defined on the group or the job.

Thoughts?

--- [conversation] 6451-7101
Comment by @timofurrer:
@cam_swords sorry, yes I mean `${{ vars.VAR_NAME }}`. I understand the reasoning of addressing variables separately and completely agree. 

> For example, `vars.XYZ` could be the mashup of variables, while `group[xyz].vars.XYZ` and `job.vars.XYZ` could return only what was explicitly defined on the group or the job.

What of this is how it's at the moment? I was assuming that this change introduces **job variables** within the `vars` object and wondered if it makes sense to align it with job inputs (in the runner, not in functions) where it might end up being `job.vars` because we already use `job.inputs` there. WDYT?

--- [conversation] 7102-8385
Comment by @cam_swords:
@timofurrer,

I'm not sure I understand what you're suggesting. Let me lay it all out, please let me know if I'm misinterpreted your suggestion :pray: 

At the moment, `job variables` both in the runner (`common.JobVariable`) and step-runner (`${{job.XYZ}}`) is the variable mashup - so more than variables defined explicitly on the job.

So for a group xyz with variables set to `A: 1`, and `B: 2`, and a job with variables `A: 3`:

**Now**

- `${{ job.A }}` evaluates to `3`
- `${{ job.B }}` evaluates to `2`

**After this MR**

- `${{ job.A }}` evaluates to `3` (backwards compatible, for now)
- `${{ job.B }}` evaluates to `2` (backwards compatible, for now)
- `${{ vars.A }}` evaluates to `3`
- `${{ vars.B }}` evaluates to `2`

**Eventually**

- `${{ job.vars.A }}` evaluates to `3`
- `${{ job.vars.B }}` errors
- `${{ group['xyz'].A }}` evaluates to `1`
- `${{ group['xyz'].B }}` evaluates to `2`
- `${{ vars.A }}` evaluates to `3`
- `${{ vars.B }}` evaluates to `2`

**What you're suggesting**

- `${{ job.vars.A }}` evaluates to `2`
- `${{ job.vars.B }}` evaluates to `3`
- Note: This is more than "job variables" (not the mashup - just those defined in the job)
- Note: expressions used by the runner should align with those used by the step-runner

=================== PR 1934   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1934
--- [title_description] 0-1402
Title: Allow user to disable default population of home directory for runner user

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Introduce a new environment variable `GITLAB_RUNNER_DISABLE_SKEL` which will disalbe the population of [`skel`](`https://www.thegeekdiary.com/understanding-the-etc-skel-directory-in-linux/`) when installing the runner through `.deb`/`.fpm` packages.

Takes into account all comments remaining from https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1523

## Why was this MR needed?

In some cases, default dotfiles cause crashes of shell runner as installed with package repositories.  
Debian 10 "Buster" notably has a .bash_logout which cause it. See forum post [here](https://forum.gitlab.com/t/gitlab-runner-as-non-root-does-not-clone-repo/21935/7) and associated issues.

I'm also waiting for this fix, even if I would prefer without a envvar and that the default would be not creating the skeleton files. As I understand it, this "breaking change" will be for the 13.0 and not a 12.x

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [x] Documentation created/updated
- [x] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [x] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

- Previous merge request !1523 
- Issues https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/1379

/cc @steveazz @marcel.amirault

--- [commits] 1403-2176
Commit messages:
Avoid populating runner home with system defaults

Fix for https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/3849

Add flag to disable skel of deb/rpm packages

The
[skel](https://www.thegeekdiary.com/understanding-the-etc-skel-directory-in-linux/)
directory might cause issues like
https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/4449 when the user
installs GitLab Runner using the `deb/rpm` repositories. Allow the user
to disable this by setting the environment variable
`GITLAB_RUNNER_DISABLE_SKEL` to `true`, so that the home directory is
created without any skeleton in it.

closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/4449

Take into account remaining comments from !1523

=================== PR 2737   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2737
--- [title_description] 0-2701
Title: Fix panic when PKCS7-encoded payload has no certificate

Description:
Some customers (ZenDesk: https://gitlab.zendesk.com/agent/tickets/191894) have certificate issuers that point to the US Federal Government CA via https://fpki.idmanagement.gov/crls/. Prior to this merge request, jobs that attempted to resolve the issuer certificate for this CA would fail:

```markdown
Checking for jobs... received job=968529 repo_url=https://gitlab.example.com/group/project.git runner=26c23a42

Processing chain chain-leaf=[0xc0009fd080] context=certificate-chain-build

<snip>
Appending the certificate to the chain Issuer= IssuerCertURL=[http://pki.treasury.gov/noca_aia.p7c] Serial=1312391446 Subject= context=certificate-chain-build method=fetchIssuerCertificate newCertIssuer= newCertIssuerCertURL=[http://pki.treasury.gov/cacertsissuedtotrca.p7c] newCertSerial=1460481024 newCertSubject=
Checking for jobs... nothing runner=Jhy85A8N
Appending the certificate to the chain Issuer= IssuerCertURL=[http://pki.treasury.gov/cacertsissuedtotrca.p7c] Serial=1460481024 Subject= context=certificate-chain-build method=fetchIssuerCertificate newCertIssuer=Federal Common Policy CA newCertIssuerCertURL=[http://http.fpki.gov/fcpca/caCertsIssuedTofcpca.p7c] newCertSerial=29515 newCertSubject=
panic: runtime error: index out of range [0] with length 0
goroutine 194 [running]:
gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/helpers/tls/ca_chain.decodeCertificate(0xc0007a6000, 0x29, 0x200, 0xc0007a6000, 0x29, 0x200)
/builds/gitlab-org/security/gitlab-runner/helpers/tls/ca_chain/helpers.go:66 +0x2be
```

This was happening because while the issuer certificate, as shown in `newCertIssuerCertURL=[http://http.fpki.gov/fcpca/caCertsIssuedTofcpca.p7c]`, has a valid PKCS7 payload, it is empty:

```sh
$ curl -O http://h [...]
--- [commits] 2702-3680
Commit messages:
Fix panic when PKCS7-encoded payload has no certificate

pkcs7.Parse() can return a nil payload if no certs were decoded. We need
to check that there are certificates before returning the first one.

Gracefully ignore issuer certificate files that are empty

Some root CAs, such as the Federal Common Policy CA, have a issuer
certificate URL encoded in PKCS7 but contains no actual certificates:

```
$ openssl pkcs7 -print_certs -in caCertsIssuedTofcpca.p7c -inform DER -noout -print
PKCS7:
  type: pkcs7-signedData (1.2.840.113549.1.7.2)
  d.sign:
    version: 1
    md_algs:
      <EMPTY>
    contents:
      type: pkcs7-data (1.2.840.113549.1.7.1)
      d.data: <ABSENT>
    cert:
      <EMPTY>
    crl:
      <EMPTY>
    signer_info:
      <EMPTY>
```

Instead of throwing up an error or crashing due to a nil access error,
we ignore this issuer certificate and stop resolving credentials. If
this root cert is trusted by the system, jobs will go through.

=================== PR 6672   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/6672
--- [title_description] 0-1483
Title: Kubernetes executor: add imagePullSecrets support for pause pods

Description:
## Summary

Add support for configuring `imagePullSecrets` for pause pods in the Kubernetes executor autoscaler.

Currently, pause pod pre-warming does not support pulling images from private registries because `imagePullSecrets` cannot be specified. This prevents usage in air-gapped and on-premise environments.

This change enables users to configure image pull secrets for pause pods.

---

## Problem

Pause pods fail to pull images from private registries because:

- `PausePodManagerConfig` did not expose `ImagePullSecrets`
- `buildDeployment()` did not set `PodSpec.ImagePullSecrets`
- No config option existed to pass secrets

---

## Solution

### Added support for:

- Configuring image pull secrets via runner config
- Passing secrets through autoscaler → manager → pod spec

---

## Changes

- Added `PausePodImagePullSecrets` to `KubernetesAutoscalerConfig`
- Wired config through `provider.go` into `PausePodManagerConfig`
- Added `ImagePullSecrets` to `PausePodManagerConfig`
- Updated `buildDeployment()` to set `PodSpec.ImagePullSecrets`
- Added helper to convert `[]string` → `[]LocalObjectReference`
- Added unit test coverage for `ImagePullSecrets`

---

## Example configuration

```toml
[runners.kubernetes.autoscaler]
  pause_pod_image = "internal-registry.example.com/pause:latest"
  pause_pod_image_pull_secrets = ["internal-registry-secret"]
```

## **Closes**: #39395

--- [commits] 1484-3420
Commit messages:
Short Description
kubernetes: add imagePullSecrets support for pause pods

Long Description
Add support for configuring imagePullSecrets for pause pods in the Kubernetes executor autoscaler.

Currently, pause pod pre-warming does not support pulling images from private registries because imagePullSecrets cannot be specified. This limits usability in air-gapped and on-premise environments where access to public registries is restricted.

This change introduces a new configuration field:

  pause_pod_image_pull_secrets

under the Kubernetes autoscaler configuration, allowing users to specify one or more Kubernetes image pull secrets for pause pod images.

Implementation details:
- Added PausePodImagePullSecrets to KubernetesAutoscalerConfig
- Wired the configuration through provider.go into PausePodManagerConfig
- Added ImagePullSecrets to PausePodManagerConfig
- Updated pause pod Deployment PodSpec to include ImagePullSecrets
- Added helper function to convert string names to LocalObjectReference
- Added unit test coverage for PodSpec imagePullSecrets

This change is fully backward compatible. If no secrets are provided, behavior remains unchanged.

Example configuration:

  [runners.kubernetes.autoscaler]
    pause_pod_image = "internal-registry.example.com/pause:latest"
    pause_pod_image_pull_secrets = ["internal-registry-secret"]

Closes issue: Add imagePullSecrets support for pause pods

Title
	Add imagePullSecrets support for Kubernetes pause pods
Description
	Adds pause_pod_image_pull_secrets support for Kubernetes executor pause pod pre-warming.

This lets pause pod images be pulled from private registries in air-gapped or on-premise deployments.

Example:

[runners.kubernetes.autoscaler]
  pause_pod_image = "internal-registry.example.com/pause: [...]

=================== PR 106   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/106
--- [title_description] 0-1863
Title: Add options to runner configuration to specify commands executed before code clone and build

Description:
This is somewhat similar to !20 but meets a need that I have in my CI setup. I'm running some of my builds on cloud VMs using the new autoscale feature. However, those are across a WAN from my GitLab instance. Some of the repositories that I do builds on are quite large, so the time required to just clone the code adds large overhead to the build process.

I've mitigated this by setting up a caching proxy server on the cloud network using [gitpod](https://github.com/sitaramc/gitpod). This works great, but I need a way to configure those runners to use the proxy Git server instead.

That's where this merge request comes in. I added a new per-runner option that allows the user to inject commands before the Git clone/fetch occurs. This allows me to adjust the Git global configuration to rewrite the fetch URLs that the CI script uses to point to my proxy server instead. This seems to work and seems to be a pretty elegant solution.

I then had to add a second option that allows the specification of commands before the build occurs. This was because my project has submodules in it, which are not currently cloneable by `gitlab-runner`. In my `.gitlab-ci.yml`, I have some commands to insert an SSH private key from a secure variable into the environment and subsequently clone the code. Since I use the Docker executor, the `git clone` occurs in a different container than the subsequent build commands, so my pre-clone commands don't have any effect on the build. Therefore, I added the ability to configure commands run at this point as well.

I know there was some opposition to adding functionality that was like this in !20, but I really believe this is the best [...]

=================== PR 7545   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/7545
--- [title_description] 0-722
Title: Fail fast when the runtime cannot join the pod user namespace

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Detects the gVisor error `error setting namespace of type user` on pod sandbox creation and fails the job immediately with a configuration error.

## Why was this MR needed?

With `hostUsers: false` on gVisor and containerd 2.2.7 or later, the pod sandbox can never start. The runner did not recognise this error, so the job waited in `Pending` until `poll_timeout` before showing the user an error.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

`go test ./executors/kubernetes/ -run TestWaitForPodRunning`

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Related to https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/work_items/597038

=================== PR 2913   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2913
--- [title_description] 0-1120
Title: Add User config setting for docker executor

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This MR let people set a user for the docker executor. All the commands executed in the container will be executed as that user.

## Why was this MR needed?

This is the first step needed to achieve https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/27895.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

(Note: This MR is intended to be used on MS Windows, but the test requirements on Linux is simpler and the interface is cross-platform. )

First, set the user field to `www-data`, which would look like this:

```toml
[[runners]]
  executor = "docker"
  builds_dir = "/tmp" # we are not root now, we have to be careful of permission issues
  # ...
  [runners.docker]
    # ...
    user = "www-data"
```

Then execute the following CI pipeline:

```yaml
stages:
  - test
test-user-change:
  stage: test
  tags: # adjust on your needs
    - os::linux
    - env::docker
  image: "php:apache"
  script:
    - bash -c "whoami"
```

The log should contain `www-data` instead of `root`.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

#27895

=================== PR 4037   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4037
--- [title_description] 0-2033
Title: Experiment: Add reviewer roulette

Description:
## What does this MR do?

**This is an experiment:** it's not a process we already committed to, it's very much up for discussions and feedback. And we **don't have to follow roulette suggestions**.

We currently depend on each developer knowing the domain expert for their specific MR and assigning them directly. We also use different tooling, see more in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/30915+ to assist with this, but it runs locally on every developer machine.

This MR introduces the standard [gitlab roulette](https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/development/code_review.html#reviewer-roulette) with all it's existing features, such as:
1. Tracking PTO/busy status.
2. WIP limits via :two:/:nine: in the status
3. using team.yml as a source for project reviewers/maintainers
etc...

I took my previous changes from https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-pages/-/merge_requests/729+ and https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-pages/-/merge_requests/732+ as the base for these changes.

## Why was this MR needed?

I see a few potential advantages of this approach:
1. it's super easy for new contributors (like me :joy:) to find the reviewer for simple changes.
2. we potentially may ask people from other parts of gitlab to become reviewers for runner. This can help a lot with the reviewer workload being distributed over a large pool of reviewers.
3. it does have some prebuilt mechanisms for distributing the load equally

Some potential disadvantages:
1. we currently require only a single approval from the runner maintainer, and don't have 2-stage reviews. And roulette assumes 2-stage reviews as default.
2. if we ask people from other teams to review our changes, then they may not spot problems early and significant re-wo [...]

Description:

=================== PR 4206   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4206
--- [title_description] 0-3274
Title: Build git-lfs from source in runner ubi-fips image

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This MR builds `git-lfs` from source in the runner `ubi-fips` docker images.

## Why was this MR needed?

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/31065 reports a whole pile of vulnerabilities against the runner:ubi-fips image, many of which are against git-lfs (see https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/31065#note_1459094800). The issue specifically is that git-lfs is build with an (old) version of go which confers vulnerabilities onto the git-lfs binary. Since we're already using the latest version of git-lfs (3.3.0 as of July 2023), we have to build it from source, with a new-enough version of go in which the vulnerabilities have been fixed.

Now, why the atrocity that is this MR? The nice way to handle this (and other manually installed dependencies) would be to create a separate docker image that manually installs/builds/whatever the required dependencies in a `scratch` image. Then downstream images that want to consume these dependencies could just do:

    COPY --from=runner-deps /source /dest

The problem with this is that runner images exist for a number of architectures and base OS images, so we'd actually need a whole lot of these dependency images. The idea still has merit, but is a much bigger job than the scope of 31065 allows.

Anyway, add to the above that a single script installs all these manual dependencies. This script, install_deps, is parametrised by TARGETPLATFORM to download the right version of the various packages it installs, AND runs in the same image as the final target image. So for
example, for runner alpine images, the install_deps scripts run in alpine images, for runner ubuntu images, the script runs in an ubuntu i [...]

=================== PR 5692   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5692
--- [title_description] 0-723
Title: Add `-depth` option to `find -exec rm` invocations

Description:
Since find with -delete is not portable, we instead invoke `find` with `-exec` and `rm * {}`. it looks like this is (alone) might not be strictly portable either, since the order in which dirs are processed could lead `find` to search in a directory that has already been deleted.

See https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/ops-sub-department/zos-runner/phase-1-certification/-/issues/12#note_2594710326

The fix appears to be as simple as adding the `-depth` options to find, which forces it to process each directory's contents in depth-first order.

Maybe fixes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/ops-sub-department/zos-runner/phase-1-certification/-/issues/12

--- [commits] 724-1317
Commit messages:
Add -depth option to `find -exec rm` invocations

Since find with -delete is not portable, we instead invoke find with
-exec and rm * {}. Looks like this is (alone) might not be strictly
portable, since the order in which dirs are processed could lead find to
search in a directory that has already been deleted.

See https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/ops-sub-department/zos-runner/phase-1-certification/-/issues/12#note_2594710326

The fix appears to be as simple as adding the `-depth` options to find,
which forces it to process each directory's contents in depth-first
order.

Comment by @kmcgreg-ibm:
FYI, this would only be required when using the `;` `find -exec` terminator as the `+` terminator causes only one `rm -rf` command to be run at the end of `find`s search.

Switching to the `;` `find -exec` terminator was a change made only on the z/OS source (at this time) as the `+` terminator is unsupported on z/OS.
It shouldn't cause problems as used here, though.

=================== PR 1189   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1189
--- [title_description] 0-1088
Title: Check either ntpdate command exists or not before trying to execute it

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This MR is successor of the !1145 MR which fixed #3666 issue.

Original fix has a minor flaw, it tries to execute `ntpdate` many times before switching to `sntp`. As result job execution delay is introduced.

This MR improves the situation. Instead of trying to execute `ntpdate` command it is suggested to check either this command exists or not, if it does not exist we immediately switch to `sntp`.

The fix has been tested with the following guest OSes:
1. Mavericks (10.9)
1. Sierra (10.12)
1. Mojave (10.14)

## Why was this MR needed?

Without these changes the Runner spends at least 20 seconds on useless spinning trying to execute non-existing `ntpdate` command on Mojave and newer macOS versions.

## Are there points in the code the reviewer needs to double check?

\-

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?



- [x] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

#3666

<details>
<summary> `git diff` </summary>

```diff
diff --git a/executors/parallels/executor_parallels.go b/executors/parallels/executor_parallels.go
index b73b2b285..4029ca942 100644

  -r xxxxxx \
  --tag-list foo \
  --executor virtualbox \

concurrent = 1

[[runners]]

=================== PR 7018   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/7018
--- [title_description] 0-1055
Title: Ensure ShortID starts with alphanumeric character for Kubernetes compatibility (closes #39456)

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Sanitises the output of `ShortenToken()` to ensure the shortened string starts with an alphanumeric character. If the first character is not alphanumeric (e.g. a dash), it is replaced with `r`.

## Why was this MR needed?

Kubernetes labels and resource names require identifiers to start with an alphanumeric character. When a CI token happened to produce a shortened ID beginning with a dash (e.g. `-abc123`), the runner generated invalid pod names that Kubernetes would reject. This was observed with the Instance and Kubernetes executors where `ShortID` values are used in resource naming.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Existing tests in `helpers/shorten_token_test.go` verify the output format. The sanitisation is a single pass over the first character — any token producing a leading non-alphanumeric char now gets `r` prepended instead.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #39456

--- [commits] 1056-1249
Commit messages:
fix: ensure ShortID starts with alphanumeric character for Kubernetes compatibility (closes #39456)

fix: sanitise trailing character and fix short-input panic in ShortenToken

--- [review_thread] 3680-5117
Review thread on helpers/shorten_token.go:
@lachie-gitlab: This only sanitises the **leading** character, but the comment right above it says Kubernetes labels must start *and end* with `[A-Za-z0-9]`. The consumer of this value (`RunnerShortToken`) is used as a raw label value in `executors/kubernetes/autoscaler/pause_pod_manager.go` (`runnerIDLabel: m.config.RunnerShortToken`), with no further sanitisation. An existing test case in `shorten_token_test.go` already produces a value ending in `-` (`"GR134894-196cJVywzZpx_Ki_Cn2" -> "GR134894-"`), which would still be an invalid Kubernetes label value after this fix. Should the last byte be checked/replaced the same way the first one is here?
@lachie-gitlab: This still isn't addressed as of the latest push (2db013dd). `ShortenToken` only checks `result[0]`:

```go
if len(result) > 0 && !isAlphaNum(result[0]) {
    result = "r" + result[:shortTokenLen-1]
}
```

There's no check on the last byte, and the exact case I flagged is still in the test suite unchanged, still asserting an invalid trailing character:

```go
{"GR134894-196cJVywzZpx_Ki_Cn2", "GR134894-"},
```

That output still ends in `-`, which per the comment above the code violates the requirement that Kubernetes label values start *and end* with `[A-Za-z0-9]`. Requesting changes until the trailing character is also sanitised (and a test added for it).
@takax: Agreed, this needs to be addressed before merge.

Review thread on helpers/shorten_token.go:
@takax: `shortTokenLen` is the truncation limit, not the length of `result`. When `result` is
shorter than 9 characters this slices out of range:

```
"-abc"      => panic: slice bounds out of range [:8] with length 4
"glrt--abc" => panic: slice bounds out of range [:8] with length 4
"_x"        => panic: slice bounds out of range [:8] with length 2
```

Should be `result[:len(result)-1]`.

On severity: GitLab-issued tokens are `glrt-` plus 20 characters, so a well-formed
token never reaches this. Unvalidated input does, though. `RunnerByToken`
(`common/config.go:2724`) calls `ShortenToken` on its argument in the not-found error
path, and that argument is the raw `--token` CLI value from
`commands/unregister.go:53`, validated only as `minLength=1`. So
`gitlab-runner unregister --token _x` panics instead of printing "could not find a
runner".

All five new cases in the test table are 9+ characters, which is why CI stays green.
Could you add a short-token case with the fix?

=================== PR 3958   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3958
--- [title_description] 0-1147
Title: Address vulnerability reports against runner-helper alpine images

Description:
Address vulnerability reports against _applications only_ in `runner-helper` `alpine` images. Vulnerability reports against `runner-helper` go modules will be addressed elsewhere.

See the issue for a full list of the vulnerabilities reported, but is summary they are against the following applications:
* busybox
* git
* libssl
* lubcurl
* git-lfs

This included a number of `critical` and `high` reports.

This MR addressed the vulnerabilities by installing the latest (or at least very new) versions of the relevant applications/libraries, using two approaches.
1. for all of the above applications/libs excluding `git-lfs`, we use an alternate package repository as suggested [here](https://github.com/alpinelinux/docker-alpine/issues/304). This repo includes newer versions of `git`, `libcurl`, `libssl`, `busybox`, and their dependencies.
2. for `git-lfs`, we install version `3.3.0` (the latest as of today) from the [official pre-compiled binary distribution](https://git-lfs.com/).  

closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29642

* libcurl-7.88.1-r1.apk (required by git)

=================== PR 6333   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/6333
--- [title_description] 0-1212
Title: Restore environment variables to build container

Description:
## What does this MR do?

* Restore environment variables to build container by fixing
a breaking change from MR !5892 that removed environment variables
from the build container when step is used. This breaks
users with custom entrypoints that depend on these variables.
Environment variables are now always set on the container config,
allowing custom entrypoints to work as expected.

* Add RUNNER_JOB_VAR_NAMES environment variable to build container containing
gzip-compressed list of job variable names. This enables step-runner to
identify and filter job variables from the OS environment, preventing
environment variable size limit issues on systems like Windows.

* Update step-runner to v0.27.0 to take in account the changes made in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/merge_requests/365+

## Why was this MR needed?

Prevent a ~"breaking change" with step-runner usage and provide a way to remove those job variables from step-runner environment variables.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Pipeline passes

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

close https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/step-runner/-/work_items/329

--- [commits] 1213-1359
Commit messages:

Update step-runner to v0.27.0

--- [conversation] 15023-16124
Comment by @cam_swords:
AI conversation, question:
```
Let's say I want to find all the environment variable names on an OS. I concatenate them separated by ;. I gzip them, and then base64 the result. 

What's the likelihood that I hit an environment length limit?
```

Response:
```
The likelihood is extremely low — you'd almost certainly be fine. Here's why:
Size Estimation
Typical system:

~50-100 environment variable names
Average name length: 10-20 characters
Raw concatenated: ~1,500-2,000 bytes

After compression and encoding:

Gzip compression (environment names compress very well due to common prefixes like PATH, PS1, etc.): ~60-70% reduction → 500-800 bytes
Base64 encoding (+33% overhead): ~650-1,070 bytes

Even in extreme cases:

500 variables × 30 chars average = 15,000 bytes raw
Compressed: ~5,000-7,500 bytes
Base64: ~6,500-10,000 bytes

Limits Comparison

Linux: 128KB total environment → Your compressed list uses <1% even in extreme cases
Windows: 32,767 chars per variable → Still only ~20-30% of the limit in extreme cases
```

If AI says it, it must be true! :joy: :tada:

One related thing though, which is that we'll need the same kind of functionality in other executors. For example, the shell executor will also need to separate job and environment variables.

If there's a better place to put it for now great, otherwise, this MR is fab.

=================== PR 1583   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1583
--- [title_description] 0-1018
Title: Extend custom executor config

Description:
## What does this MR do?

1. Extends the Custom Executor `config` API with two new information that can be passed to the Runner: customized hostname of the environment where the job is executed and the information about the driver (currently name and version).

1. Moves the `ConfigExecOutput` structure to a separate package - `gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/executors/custom/api` - so the drivers may import it and use it for preparing the configuration output.

## Why was this MR needed?

These changes came up while working on our initial ~"Shared Runners::Windows" implementation with the https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/ci-cd/custom-executors/autoscaler driver.

## Are there points in the code the reviewer needs to double check?

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [ ] Documentation created/updated
- [ ] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [ ] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

--- [commits] 1019-1252

--- [review_thread] 9228-10237
Review thread on executors/custom/api/config.go:
@sxuereb: Do you think this warrant a package for just 22 lines of code? I feel like having it under `executor/custom` is enough since it's 1 simple struct for now I also feel like this should be part of `ConfigExecOutput` as it was orginally.
@tmaczukin: The whole idea of putting this into a separate package is that we can import such package in driver implementations (e.g. https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/ci-cd/custom-executors/autoscaler/blob/f16e4834dbda4b4864e28c9bee06c9b205663087/internal/runner/adapter.go#L10). We definitely don't want to import full `executor/custom` in that case.

Look on this like on the `api "k8s.io/api/core/v1"` import in Runner's Kubernetes executor. The package contains structs that the client software can import and use to prepare requests that will be send to Kubernetes API. But the client software don't need and even should not import the packages that contain the internal k8s implementation that uses such structs.

=================== PR 1754   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1754
--- [title_description] 0-629
Title: Only run image build jobs on main repo

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Only run image build jobs on main repo

## Why was this MR needed?

When a fork tries to run these jobs it leads to a failure becuase they
don't have access to the container registry when the job tries to push
the images. For example
https://gitlab.com/steve.exley/gitlab-runner-ff2/pipelines/107997689

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [x] Documentation created/updated
- [x] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [x] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #6261

--- [commits] 630-986
Commit messages:
Only run image build jobs on main repo

When a fork tries to run these jobs it leads to a failure becuase they
don't have access to the container registry when the job tries to push
the images. For example
https://gitlab.com/steve.exley/gitlab-runner-ff2/pipelines/107997689

closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/6261

=================== PR 2211   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2211
--- [title_description] 0-807
Title: Remove redundant docker executor integration tests

Description:
## What does this MR do?

The assertion of the `after_script` being executed was introduced in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1963/diffs#438aa692e2ac638824399e1591cbd7c150f61f29_80_81 with `TestDockerCommandMultistepBuild`.

This MR removes the tests `TestDockerCommandBuildFail` and `TestDockerCommandSuccessRun` which are included in the test mentioned above.

Closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/25425.

## Why was this MR needed?

`TestDockerCommandMultistepBuild` runs the same assertions as `TestDockerCommandBuildFail` and `TestDockerCommandSuccessRun` so those tests are redundant.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

 #25425

=================== PR 2223   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2223
--- [title_description] 0-527
Title: Use newest helper image version in tests

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Makes tests use the newest helper image tag that goes alongside the pushed code, instead of `latest` that is associated with the previous release.

## Why was this MR needed?

We can't run integration tests relying on new functionality in the helper image when using the `latest` tag.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Follow the instructions in `docs/development/README.md`

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #25652

=================== PR 5745   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5745
--- [title_description] 0-618
Title: Enable image executor opts in the kubernetes executor

Description:
https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/38942+ reports that they cannot set the `user` image executor opts in the ~"executor::kubernetes". But, but, we added support for than in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5469+. But, but, we did not enable it by reporting to gitlab that the executor supports that feature :frowning2:. 

Note that the first commit just sorts the features alphabetically for easier comparison, so can be skipped.

Fixes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/38942+

=================== PR 2227   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2227
--- [title_description] 0-788
Title: Fix missing build_script step for custom executor

Description:
## What does this MR do?
Translates `"step_script"` to `"build_script"` stage for the custom executor.

## Why was this MR needed?

This is needed as the stage names are in a sense a public API and https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1963 broke it by introducing a new stage, which is not referenced in the documentation: https://docs.gitlab.com/13.1/runner/executors/custom.html#run

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Using a custom executor which relies on "build_script" as a stage name, for example the one referenced in gitlab-org/gitlab-runner#26418

The integration test is a good example of that.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes gitlab-org/gitlab-runner#26418

=================== PR 3246   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3246
--- [title_description] 0-714
Title: Add a flag to `gitlab-runner exec` to specify the CI/CD config file

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Add a flag to `gitlab-runner exec` to specify the CI/CD config file (`.gitlab-ci.yml` by default), similar to [what's available in the GitLab Pipeline configuration panel](https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/ci/pipelines/settings.html#specify-a-custom-cicd-configuration-file).

## Why was this MR needed?

Right now there is no way to run `gitlab-runner exec` on a project using a CI configuration file that's not called `.gitlab-ci.yml`.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

```sh
gitlab-runner exec docker job-name --cicd-config-file ./path/to/config.yml
```

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

=================== PR 4379   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4379
--- [title_description] 0-2099
Title: Manually refresh JobVariables prior to ConfigExec

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This modification aims to ensure that the ConfigExec stage is always
provided with the most up to date JobVariables when attempting to
construct the environment for the subsequent command.

## Why was this MR needed?

The issues https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29660 seems to be caused by cases where the [GetAllVariables](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/common/build.go?ref_type=heads#L1170) is called while preparing a command in the [dynamicConfig](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/executors/custom/custom.go?ref_type=heads#L164); however, since `b.allVariables` is not `nil` changes to default variables are not captured in the variables returned. This is most apparent with the `CI_CONCURRENT_ID` and `CI_CONCURRENT_PROJECT_ID` variables since they can be relied upon by custom executor drivers to construct the `builds_dir`.

I want to note that I'm not confident enough in my understanding of all the executors so I've tried to keep the change focused only on the custom executor. If I'm wrong and this behavior is negatively affecting other types I could rethink the modification to occur before the `func (e *executor) Prepare...` is invoked for any of the executors.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

At the moment the easiest path to test this is by manually creating a simple custom executor that can concurrently run at least two jobs. Then you can include a bash script like the one below for the `config_exec`:  

```bash
#!/bin/bash
mkdir -p "/tmp/${CUSTOM_ENV_CI_JOB_ID}"
env >> "/tmp/${CUSTOM_ENV_CI_JOB_ID}/env"
```

Running a pipeline with multiple concurrent jobs without the changes you'll be able to note [...]

Comment by @paulbry:
Thanks @avonbertoldi 

I found it easiest to observe with a custom executor with at least a `concurrent` value great then one:

```toml
concurrent = 2
check_interval = 0
shutdown_timeout = 0

[session_server]
  session_timeout = 1800

[[runners]]
  name = "mr-test"
  executor = "custom"
  builds_dir = "/tmp"
  cache_dir = "/tmp"
  ...
  [runners.custom]
    config_exec = "/etc/gitlab-runner/cfg.bash"
    run_exec = "/etc/gitlab-runner/exit.bash"
```

For `cfg.bash`:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
mkdir -p "/tmp/${CUSTOM_ENV_CI_JOB_ID}"
env >> "/tmp/${CUSTOM_ENV_CI_JOB_ID}/env"
```

With `exit.bash` is just a script that `exit 0` since the `run_exec` is required by the runner in order to operate but I didn't really need it for testing.

So long as the pipeline you run has multiple jobs running in parallel the dump of the environment variables can be used to see what the `CI_CONCURRENT_PROJECT_ID` is. With these changes it should have different values so when they are used to construct the build directory it remains unique. However, with the current runner release they will both be `0`.

If it help I can also add a unit test.

=================== PR 3528   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3528
--- [title_description] 0-1717
Title: Push image on registry during release stage only when enabled

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This MR allows images to be pushed on the registry only if enabled

## Why was this MR needed?

At the moment, the GitLab Runner images published on the [AWS ECR registry have been deactivated (PUSH_TO_ECR_PUBLIC: false)](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/a5c3c6a338d8bb21827a53a9c831b8512d352278/.gitlab/ci/release.gitlab-ci.yml#L10). However, the CI still tries to push to images resulting in the failure of the related job as it can be seen in the `Release` stage of those pipelines (bleeding edge helper docker images jobs):

* https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/pipelines/588444776
* https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/pipelines/588395208

This MR fixes the issue so the pipeline doesn't fail anymore.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

To test this MR, the following variables have been updated in the `Release` stage for the `development helper docker images` jobs.

* `PUBLISH_IMAGES` set to `true`
* `PUSH_TO_ECR_PUBLIC` kept to `false`

PS: The error wasn't occurring in the `development helper docker images` jobs because of `PUBLISH_IMAGES` set to `false`

[Test pipeline](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/pipelines/588833789) and [related commit](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/commit/ba2d88b64f673c06f88eb7cd4075ea74f3312108)

In the following logs, we can see that there is no failure caused by an attempt to push on AWS ECR

* https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/jobs/2726969516#L320
* https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/jobs/2726969517#L307

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

close #29170

Commit messages:
Check push status before taking action

=================== PR 2461   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2461
--- [title_description] 0-693
Title: Allow specifying `basefolder` when creating virtualbox VM

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Allows specifying the base folder in which to create new virtualbox VMs

## Why was this MR needed?

In our setup, we store the VMs across multiple SSDs to avoid bottlenecking when there are a large number of jobs running simultaneously. This allows us to automatically split the VMs across the drives by specifying a different target location for each base image.

The [`clonevm` command](https://www.virtualbox.org/manual/ch08.html#vboxmanage-clonevm) already supports the `--basefolder` flag to achieve this, this MR simply adds the ability to configure this flag from the config.toml

--- [commits] 694-839
Commit messages:

=================== PR 4218   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4218
--- [title_description] 0-499
Title: Prune tags when fetching

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Add the pruning tags at git fetch level.

## Why was this MR needed?

CI/CD jobs playing with tags (like semantic-release) need an effective status of the tags. If some of them were removed between two pipelines, the removed ones should not remain in the local copy.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Create a tag
2. Run CI job displaying tags
3. Remove the tag
4. Same as 2

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

--- [commits] 500-979
Commit messages:
Prune tags when fetching

If a git project set tags and finally remove them, the tags will remain on local git repository.
This could be annoying with CI/CD jobs preparing releases and inspecting tags (like semantic-release).

Pruning the tags is needed.

When pruning both tags and branch, listing tags refmap seems mandatory while `--prune-tags` option
does not work.

Co-Author: @avonbertoldi
Signed-off-by: Guilhem Bonnefille <guilhem.bonnefille@csgroup.eu>

--- [conversation] 5888-6526
Comment by @avonbertoldi:
@gbonnefille OK, I think I may have figured out why `--prune-tags` doesn't behave as expected (and possibly `--prune` too). Runner's `fetch` command is something like this:

```sh
git -c 'http.userAgent=gitlab-runner development version linux/amd64' fetch origin +refs/pipelines/<PIPELINE-ID>:refs/pipelines/<PIPELINE-ID> +refs/heads/<BRANCH>:refs/remotes/origin/<BRANCH> --depth 20 --prune --prune-tags --quiet
```

It looks like the specifying a `refmap` (the `+refs` bits) effectively disables `--prune-tags`. If I remove the `refmap`, tags are pruned as expected. Continuing to explore why that is the case.

--- [conversation] 6527-6736
Comment by @avonbertoldi:
Adding this refmap seems to make it all work `+refs/tags/*:refs/tags/*`. For example this works:
```yaml
variables:
  GIT_FETCH_EXTRA_FLAGS: +refs/tags/*:refs/tags/* --prune-tags
```

--- [conversation] 12626-13236
Comment by @avonbertoldi:
@gbonnefille The thing is, because of the explicit refspecs in runner's `fetch` incovations, `tags` should never be fetched in the first place. I'd suggest instead the if a job is creating tags (and possibly pushing them to origin), maybe it should be responsible for cleaning them up, either in `before_script` or `after_script`. Would that work? This could be "templated" in the pipeline config with `extends` keyword.

=================== PR 5846   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5846
--- [title_description] 0-735
Title: Add project name to build logging fields

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Includes more information about the job context (project full path, namespace/root namespace/organization IDs, triggering user ID) in the runner logs.

## Why was this MR needed?

Currently we only log project id, which requires a separate lookup (or checking the repo url) to figure out which project a job is running on. Additional contex makes debugging easier. And we will also use the same data in a follow-up MR to extend the information provided by GitLab Runner Usage Logs.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

refs https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/gl-infra/production-engineering/-/issues/27692

--- [commits] 736-1560
Commit messages:
Add project name to build logging fields

Include project_name field alongside project ID in build logs for better visibility and debugging.

Add enhanced project and user metadata to runner job logging

- Replace project_name with project_full_path for better namespace visibility
- Add user_id and username fields to track job initiators
- Include optional scoped_user_id and scoped_username for SAML/group-scoped users
- Extend JobInfo struct to support the new metadata fields from GitLab API

This improves observability by providing complete context about job origins,
making it easier to identify and debug issues related to specific projects
and users in multi-tenant runner environments.

Add namespace, root namespace and organization info to job payload

Usage new payload data in runner logging

=================== PR 3831   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3831
--- [title_description] 0-603
Title: Powershell: fix unwanted progress streams leaking to output

Description:
## What does this MR do?

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3707
forced all commands to be encoded in base64 to avoid UTF-8 encoding
issues.

However, this caused unwanted progress output to be leaked with
each command due to an upstream bug:
https://github.com/PowerShell/PowerShell/issues/5912.

This commit reverts he previous behavior of using unencoded commands
for `powershell`, but leaves in the encoding for `pwsh`.

Relates to https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/29462

=================== PR 6695   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/6695
--- [title_description] 0-2171
Title: Pass proactiveAuth to git checkout for partial-clone lazy fetches

Description:
## What does this MR do?

When `FF_USE_GIT_PROACTIVE_AUTH` is enabled, pass `-c http.proactiveAuth=basic` to `git checkout` in addition to `git clone` and `git fetch`.

In partial-clone repositories, `git checkout` can trigger a lazy fetch from the promisor remote to retrieve missing objects. That lazy fetch is a separate HTTP request that does not inherit `-c` flags from the original clone/fetch invocation, so the username is not propagated to Gitaly during those fetches.

Changes:
- `shells/abstract.go` (`writeCheckoutCmd`): build checkout args dynamically, conditionally including `http.proactiveAuth=basic`
- `functions/concrete/run/stages/get_sources.go` (`gitCheckout`): same change for the functions path
- `shells/abstract_test.go`: add `TestGitProactiveAuthCheckout` covering enabled/disabled cases

## Why was this MR needed?

See [this discussion](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/work_items/591939#note_3303469285) (internal link)

`writeCheckoutCmd` and `gitCheckout` hard-code only `-c submodule.recurse=false` and never check the `UseGitProactiveAuth` feature flag. The clone and fetch paths already pass this flag, but checkout was missed. This means that in partial-clone setups where checkout triggers a lazy fetch, the proactiveAuth setting is not applied, and the username is not propagated to Gitaly.

Observed in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/jobs/14101195927 where `FF_USE_GIT_PROACTIVE_AUTH:true` was set and a lazy fetch during checkout failed without the proactiveAuth config.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Run the new `TestGitProactiveAuthCheckout` unit test:
   ```
   go test -run TestGitProactiveAuthCheckout ./shells/ -count=1
   ```
2. Verify ex [...]
--- [commits] 2172-3093
Commit messages:
Pass proactiveAuth to git checkout for partial-clone lazy fetches

When FF_USE_GIT_PROACTIVE_AUTH is enabled, the runner passes
`-c http.proactiveAuth=basic` to `git clone` and `git fetch`, but not
to `git checkout`. In partial-clone repositories, `git checkout` can
trigger a lazy fetch from the promisor remote to retrieve missing
objects. That lazy fetch does not inherit `-c` flags from the original
clone/fetch invocation, so the username is not propagated to Gitaly.

Root cause: writeCheckoutCmd and gitCheckout hard-code only
`-c submodule.recurse=false` and never check the UseGitProactiveAuth
feature flag.

Prepend `-c http.proactiveAuth=basic` to the checkout args when the
feature flag is enabled, matching the pattern already used in
writeCloneRevisionCmd and writeRefspecFetchCmd. Apply the same fix to
the functions/concrete path (gitCheckout).

Construct checkoutArgs in a single append

=================== PR 1647   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1647
--- [title_description] 0-1310
Title: Change config lock to create a separate lock file

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Change config lock to create a separate lock file

## Why was this MR needed?

When creating a lock create a new lock file, for example
`config.toml.lock` to prevent `gitlab-runner run` process using the same
config file, and fix issues for Windows where there is only exclusive
locks (so no other process can even read from it).

With this change, we still end up allowing users using the same config
file when they just mount the `config.toml` file to a Docker container
since the lock file is not mounted so we lose that functionality. This
is expected and is a more complex thing to fix, which a file lock is not
enough, that kind of locking should happen on GitLab coordinator side.

## Testing

### Windows

- Run runner process
- Try to run another runner process, which fails :white_check_mark: 
- Register new runner :white_check_mark: 

![Screen_Shot_2019-10-29_at_13.31.33](/uploads/930620092a01b7d19b7b0eacd6f5dde2/Screen_Shot_2019-10-29_at_13.31.33.png)

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [ ] Documentation created/updated
- [ ] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [ ] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

closes #4691 #4721

=================== PR 4145   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4145
--- [title_description] 0-947
Title: Mark ConfigExecTimeout as optional

Description:
Problem: when running `gitlab-runner list` with `[runners.custom]` section that has no `config_exec_timeout` field defined, GitLab Runner throws the following warning:

```
There might be a problem with your config based on jsonschema annotations in common/config.go (experimental feature):
jsonschema: '/runners/0/custom/config_exec_timeout' does not validate with https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/common/config#/$ref/properties/runners/items/$ref/properties/custom/$ref/properties/config_exec_timeout/type: expected integer, but got null
```

However, GitLab Runner can actually handle this value being empty, see https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/fa52677c2bba605c4971a6d61ddabc66b1690b3d/executors/custom/config.go#L14-16.

Solution: mark `ConfigExecTimeout` as `omitempty`, similarly to how `PrepareExecTimeout` and `CleanupExecTimeout` are marked currently.

=================== PR 2555   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2555
--- [title_description] 0-1720
Title: Pin StefanScherer/windows_2019_docker box to 2020.04.15

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Pins the Windows vagrant base box to [`2020.04.15`](https://app.vagrantup.com/StefanScherer/boxes/windows_2019_docker/versions/2020.04.15).

## Why was this MR needed?

Later versions of the `StefanScherer/windows_2019_docker` box have upgraded Docker to a version (more recent than 19.03.05) that doesn't deal well with the `C:\GitLab-Runner` shared volume symlink.

`.\ci\build_release_windows_images.ps1` was failing with the following error:

```
unable to prepare context: unable to evaluate symlinks in context path: CreateFile \\vboxsvr\C:_GitLab-Runnerdockerfiles: The network name cannot be found.
```

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Recreate the vagrant image with:

    ```shell
    vagrant destroy -f; vagrant up
    ```

1. SSH into the vagrant VM:

    ```shell
    vagrant ssh
    ```

1. Enter a new `powershell` session.

1. Build the Windows Docker image:

    ```powershell
    cd $Env:RUNNER_SRC
    $Env:CI_REGISTRY_IMAGE="localhost:5000/gitlab"
    $Env:WINDOWS_VERSION = 'servercore1809'
    $Env:GIT_LFS_256_CHECKSUM= "5c12db9728b53cba23e5f58f4a53d88cb2132e82fa1de0f8a79ce9d112e4d396"
    $Env:GIT_256_CHECKSUM= "bd91db55bd95eaa80687df28877e2df8c8858a0266e9c67331cfddba2735f25c"
    $Env:GIT_VERSION= "2.21.0"
    $Env:GIT_VERSION_BUILD= "1"

=================== PR 3960   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3960
--- [title_description] 0-893
Title: Fix 'clear-docker-cache' script for Docker 23.0

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Fixes 'clear-docker-cache' script for Docker 23.0

## Why was this MR needed?

Docker 23.0 changed the existing `docker system prune` behaviour, and the workaround
for now is to fallback to the older behaviour by explicitly specifying the older API
version: https://github.com/docker/cli/issues/4028#issuecomment-1429538145

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Execute the `clear-docker-cache` command and check that the Runner volumes are removed when running against Docker 23.0.

```
docker run --privileged -it -v $(pwd):/scripts --rm --entrypoint sh docker:23.0.0-dind
dockerd &
```

```
apk add bash
docker volume create --label com.gitlab.gitlab-runner.managed=true with-label-named
./scripts/clear-docker-cache prune-volumes
```

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #29668

--- [review_thread] 2261-5471
Review thread on packaging/root/usr/share/gitlab-runner/clear-docker-cache:
@TobiX: Question: Is it a good idea to hardcode the API version here? Line 18 suggests this script is designed to work with Docker versions since 1.13 (see [API version matrix](https://docs.docker.com/engine/api/#api-version-matrix)). What happens if Docker is older then 19.03?

Related tangent: Since Docker versions older 20.10 aren't supported anymore (https://endoflife.date/docker-engine), the simple fix would be to raise `REQUIRED_DOCKER_API_VERSION` to 1.40 and be done with it :smile_cat: (This would also allow to remove all the compatibility code for Docker older 17.06.1)
@ajwalker: @TobiX That's a great point. This _used_ to be okay to do, but it looks like since 19.03, there might be issues with the approach.

The behaviour used to work almost like `DOCKER_API_VERSION` was suggesting the version of the daemon, and the client would fallback if it didn't support that version:


**Setting to really high API version, it gets downgraded**
```shell
$ DOCKER_API_VERSION=1.99 docker version

  OS/Arch:          linux/arm64
  Experimental:     false
```

**With newer version, 19.03**

```shell

Client: Docker Engine - Community
 Version:           19.03.15
 API version:     [...]
