=================== PR 3265   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3265
--- [title_description] 0-1476
Title: Init submodules prior to sync to ensure submodules remote URL configuration is properly synchronized

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Adds call to `git submodule init` prior to `git submodule sync` ensuring the `.git/config` file is fully initialized allowing submodules remote URL configuration to be properly synchronized when `git submodule sync` is run on a reinitialized existing Git repository having submodules.

## Why was this MR needed?

Changes introduced in !3134 caused a regression when `FF_ENABLE_JOB_CLEANUP` is enabled. See #28783 for full details.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

I tested these changes by deploying a test runner into our Kubernetes environment, locking the pods it creates to a single node to ensure the same shared storage was used from one job to the next, essentially walking through the scenario where the problem occurred as documented in steps to reproduce here: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/28783#steps-to-reproduce

We've been running a patched runner image having these changes across all our runners for about a week without issue, but would like to see the changes merged in upstream so we don't have to maintain a patched image, and so that others don't run into the same.

Might need to update with testing procedure easily executed by others if detail on related issue isn't sufficient to make it obvious what is occurring here.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

#28783

=================== PR 2971   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2971
--- [title_description] 0-984
Title: Only print necessary env vars in tests

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Only print necessary env vars in tests

## Why was this MR needed?

When using the `env` or `set` keyword it prints all the environments
that are available in the job. These can be problematic printing
environments that we shouldn't print. Update the tests to run an `echo`
on the specific variables that we are testing instead. This makes
running the tests safer since it won't print in every environment.

In !2951 we
only allow certain environment variables, however, it's better to have
multiple layers of protection rather than 1 just in case 1 of them
regresses.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Make sure that `NOT_HERE` environment variable isn't printed.

```
NOT_HERE=NOT_HERE go test -v -run TestBuildJobStatusEnvVars -tags integration ./executors/shell/ | grep 'NOT_HERE'
```

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/27963

regresses.

=================== PR 2968   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2968
--- [title_description] 0-2038
Title: Update configuration of changelog generator

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Updates the configuraiton of `gitlab-changelog`.

## Why was this MR needed?

Update configuration of changelog generator:
    
1. Change the ordering of label matchers.
   
    Labels like `documentation` or `runner-distribution` are often
    connected with other labels that we're matching.
    
    In case of `runner-distribution` it's usually connected with one
    of the maintenance labels. But as the change is also about the
    change in something related to Runner's distribution process,
    this rule should take the preference.
    
    As for `documentation`, the label is often added to the merge
    requests that have both backend code and documentation changes.
    As it's added as part of the Technical Writing review.
    However, in such case the funcional change (for example feature
    addition or tooling updates) have higher priority than the
    documentation update, so documentation was pushed further in the
    list.
    
1. Adds the `skip-changelog` label that defines a MR that should be
   skipped.
    
    From time to time we have a case, when a specific MR should not be
    included in the changelog.
    
    The most common situation is when a change is added and next
    reverted in the same release. Currently both entries would be added
    to the changelog and it's in release manager's responsibility to find
    these and manually remove them. This however should be done while
    reverting the change - both the initial and the revert MR should be
    labeled by the maintainer with the ~"skip-changelog" label so that
    the release manager doesn't need to track such cases and manually
    update the generated list.
    
    This feature will require a [...]
--- [commits] 2039-3847
Commit messages:
Update configuration of changelog generator

1. Change the ordering of label matchers.

    Labels like `documentation` or `runner-distribution` are often
    connected with other labels that we're matching.

    In case of `runner-distribution` it's usually connected with one
    of the maintenance labels. But as the change is also about the
    change in something related to Runner's distribution process,
    this rule should take the preference.

    As for `documentation`, the label is often added to the merge
    requests that have both backend code and documentation changes.
    As it's added as part of the Technical Writing review.
    However, in such case the funcional change (for example feature
    addition or tooling updates) have higher priority than the
    documentation update, so documentation was pushed further in the
    list.

1. Adds the `skip-changelog` label that defines a MR that should be
   skipped.

   From time to time we have a case, when a specific MR should not be
   included in the changelog.

   The most common situation is when a change is added and next
   reverted in the same release. Currently both entries would be added
   to the changelog and it's in release manager's responsibility to find
   these and manually remove them. This however should be done while
   reverting the change - both the initial and the revert MR should be
   labeled by the maintainer with the ~"skip-changelog" label so that
   the release manager doesn't need to track such cases and manually
   update the generated list.

   This feature will require a new version of `gitlab-changelog` that is
   being prepared in
   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/ci-cd/runner-tools/gitlab-changelog/-/merge_requests/22.

Update documentation about changelog gen [...]

=================== PR 3119   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3119
--- [title_description] 0-1022
Title: Better support for Powershell on Kubernetes

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Powershell Core supported for Kubernetes was added some time ago, because it worked for Linux.

With the introduction of Windows on Kubernetes, we also need to support Powershell, which has some subtle differences.

## Why was this MR needed?

- Powershell needs script file extensions, so we needed to add them.
- Powershell defaults to utf32 output, which means our log processing for a JSON line would fail with an unmarshaling error. The output is now utf8, so the build can terminate as expected.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

- Run a any linux job, to ensure that the extension script change continues to work.
- Run a windows job with `shell = "powershell"`. It should no longer hang indefinitely.

For the Linux job, our integration tests should already be covering this case multiple times, so it's likely only required to be tested manually on Windows.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #28153

--- [commits] 1023-1150
Commit messages:
Fix Kubernetes permissions init container for powershell

Add powershell support for Kubernetes build scripts

=================== PR 3846   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3846

Title: Fix windows integration tests failure check

Description:
Not sure how I missed this until now :face_palm: 

The rules for when `check windows xxxx integration test failures` run
were wrong. The rules for these thee jobs were:

    default-branch-only:no_docs:always

Which means they would not run on MRs, which in turn meant that new
failures wouldn't be caught until later (i.e. release or main build).
This commit will make those jobs run on MR pipelines too.

And now because of this, the two integration tests that fail on windows
will have to be added to the expected failures.

Follow-up for https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3830

=================== PR 4142   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4142
--- [title_description] 0-1185
Title: Automatically set GOMAXPROCS based on CPU Quotas

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Uses `go.uber.org/automaxprocs/maxprocs` to automatically set `GOMAXPROCS`.

`GOMAXPROCS` usually matches the number of CPU cores available for the machine. Many libraries use this to inform them of the concurrency they can use.

However, `GOMAXPROCS` still uses the number of CPU cores available for the machine even if cgroup quotas have been set, as is common for Kubernetes and Docker. This can lead to scenarios where the concurrent value is set way higher than is intended in these environments, leading to poor performance.

## Why was this MR needed?

Specifically the `fastzip` library defaults to `GOMAXPROCS`, and in Kubernetes environments where the helper binary has a CPU quota, this can lead to poor utilisation of compute and memory.

However, in general, `GOMAXPROCS` is used to inform the Go scheduler and it's better to not have that over commit.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Related to https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/402233

Might help with https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/27984

--- [commits] 1186-1252
Commit messages:
Automatically set GOMAXPROCS based on CPU Quotas

--- [conversation] 4181-4906
Comment by @ajwalker:
@gitlab-com/runner-maintainers

In this MR, I'm proposing that we automatically set `GOMAXPROCS` for Kubernetes/container based environments.

This is being discussed for other Go applications here: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/402233

Summary: GOMAXPROCS is based on the number of CPUs. But in environments where a CPU quota is set, GOMAXPROCS still matches the underlying machines core count, which can lead to performance problems, as libraries and the go scheduler uses this value to determine concurrency. This library from Uber will automatically set the correct value based on the cgroup quota. It's a no-op outside of these environments.

Does anybody disagree with doing this?

--- [conversation] 4907-4952
Comment by @josephburnett:
Seems fine to me.

--- [conversation] 4953-5121
Comment by @dbickford:
Also agreed :thumbsup: I'm a bit surprised we'd get a default `GOMAXPROCS` value matching the host VM rather than the guest VM anyway :thinking:

--- [conversation] 5122-5227
Comment by @dbickford:
Oh, I see, you mentioned cgroup. I'm caught up now :smile: Still agree :thumbsup:

=================== PR 7008   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/7008
--- [title_description] 0-664
Title: Log successful network disconnect at Info level

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Changes the log level for successful network disconnects from `WARNING` to `Info`, and removes the alarming "zombie container" wording.

Closes https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/39616

## Why?

Since commit `869d9e0e8` ("Fix disconnectNetwork container ID matching"), the `disconnectNetwork()` function actually works for the first time. However, the success branch was logging at `WARNING` level with "Possibly zombie container" wording, causing user confusion and support tickets.

A successful cleanup operation should not be logged as a warning.

--- [commits] 665-1302

The disconnectNetwork() function was logging successful disconnects at
WARNING level with alarming "zombie container" wording. This caused
user confusion since commit 869d9e0e8 made the container matching
actually work for the first time.

Change the success branch to use Infoln with neutral wording, while
keeping the failure branch as Warningln.

Strip leading slash from container name in disconnect logs

Docker container names include a leading "/" which looks odd in logs.
Use strings.TrimPrefix consistent with provider.go LogFields pattern.

=================== PR 3805   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3805
--- [title_description] 0-960
Title: Prepare register command to fail if runner server-side configuration options are passed together with a new glrt- token

Description:
## What does this MR do?

This MR prepares the `register` command to fail if the user tries to use a new `glrt-` runner token as a registration token, and at the same time tries to leverage the command line arguments to configure the runner on the server side (e.g. setting tags, paused, protected status, etc.)

## Why was this MR needed?

See second paragraph in https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/architecture/blueprints/runner_tokens/#using-the-authentication-token-in-place-of-the-registration-token

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Try to pass any of the forbidden arguments when passing a fake registration that begins with `glrt-`;
1. Try to pass the same arguments when using a normal valid registration token. This time registration should succeed.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #29510

=================== PR 3147   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3147
--- [title_description] 0-959
Title: Update golang-cli-helpers library, support env namespaces

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Allows the `namespace` setting on our config entries to take affect for `env` entries, but only when an env name is prefixed with `@`.

This MR updates the library, but doesn't make use of the new functionality yet, as although the current env names can be verbose, there's little pointing in updating them.

## Why was this MR needed?

Newer Kubernetes configuration settings would benefit from the new functionality: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3116

=================== PR 5497   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5497
--- [title_description] 0-1476
Title: Add job to rebase branches on main

Description:
## What does this MR do?

A simple automation to allow us to rebase main on top of branches each time a commit is made in main.

It's currently needed in order to rebase `feature/fault-tolerance` regularly. Together with https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5489 we can do the following workflow:

```mermaid
gitGraph
    commit id: "HEAD"
    branch "feature/fault-tolerance"
    checkout main
    commit id: "New commit"
    checkout "feature/fault-tolerance"
    commit id: "Fault tolerance implementation"
    checkout main
    checkout "feature/fault-tolerance"
    merge main id: "Rebase new commit from main"
    commit id: "New commit rebased on fault tolerance"
    commit id: "Deploy to Kubernetes" type: HIGHLIGHT
```

This gives us the opportunity to test different features in case we don't want them merged into main yet.

## Why was this MR needed?

## What's the best way to test this MR?

New pipelines from this branch can be ran with CI_COMMIT_BRANCH and CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME set to main.

This is a pipeline where the correct jobs structure can be seen (rebase job exists)

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/pipelines/1754544839

And this is an isolated pipeline where the job passed: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/pipelines/1754544790

Related to https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/38458+s

--- [review_thread] 5699-7596
Review thread on .gitlab/ci/rebase.gitlab-ci.yml:
@hoegaarden: **nitpick (mon-blocking):** 

- We might want to not bail out when one of the branches fails to auto-rebase, but still try to rebase other branches, while in the end we still exit with an error code if one branch failed.
- I think if rebasing fails, we might need to do some cleanup, before we can switch branches. So I suggest to abort the rebase and force reset the branch

This could look something like this (untested!):

```suggestion:-25+0
      set -e
      set -u
      set -o pipefail

      rc=0

      git config --global user.email "gitlab-runner-ci-rebase@gitlab.com"
      git config --global user.name "GitLab Runner CI Rebase"

      git fetch origin $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH
      git checkout -B $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH origin/$CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH

      git remote add push-remote https://oauth2:${REPO_REBASE_PUSH_TOKEN}@${CI_SERVER_HOST}/${CI_PROJECT_PATH}.git

      GIT_DIR=$( git rev-parse --git-dir )

      IFS=',' read -ra BRANCHES <<< "$REPO_REBASE_BRANCHES"
      for BRANCH in "${BRANCHES[@]}"; do
        echo "Processing branch: $BRANCH"

        git fetch origin "$BRANCH"
        git checkout "$BRANCH"
        if git rebase $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH ; then
          # --force-with-lease doesn't work on shallow clones
          git push push-remote "$1" --force
        else
          rc=$(( rc | $? ))
          echo "Rebasing $BRANCH onto $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH failed."
          if [ -d "${GIT_DIR}/rebase-merge" ] || [ -d "${GIT_DIR}/rebase-apply" ] ; then
            git rebase --abort
          fi
          git reset --hard "origin/${BRANCH}"
        fi

        git checkout $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH
      done

      exit $rc
```

=================== PR 3789   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/3789
--- [title_description] 0-1891
Title: Allow custom executor to specify the shell used

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Allows configuring the `shell` used by a custom executor in the dynamic configuration step.

## Why was this MR needed?

Currently, a given custom executor runner can only ever use a single type of shell. We have a custom executor whose job is to setup VMs in a kubevirt cluster. The way it works is simple: A user defines a job as so:

```
jobname:
    tag: kubevirt-runner
    env:
        KUBEVIRT_TEMPLATE: name-of-template
```

The runner will use the `KUBEVIRT_TEMPLATE` to create a new VM based on that template name, run the CI, and finally delete the VM.

The problem is, we have different templates, running in different OSes, and thus needing different shells: Some use cmd, others bash. In the current `gitlab-runner`, however, a given executor may only work with a single shell, hardcoded in its `config.toml`. As such, it is not possible to configure the shell dynamically based on the `KUBEVIRT_TEMPLATE`.

This makes the user-experience less ideal, as the user has to set a different tag depending on the shell they want to use.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Try the following runner:

`config.sh`:

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
echo '{"shell":"pwsh"}'
```

`run.sh`:

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
pwsh $1
```

`config.toml`:

```
concurrent = 50
check_interval = 0
[session_server]
  session_timeout = 1800
[[runners]]
  name = "custom-runner"
  url = "https://gitlab.com/"
  token = "<TOKEN>"
  executor = "custom"
  builds_dir = "/tmp/gitlab-runner/builds"
  cache_dir = "/tmp/gitlab-runner/cache"
  [runners.custom]
    config_exec = "config.sh"
    run_exec = "run.sh"
```

With this MR, `run.sh`'s first argument will be a powershell script that can be ran through powershell.

=================== PR 6848   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/6848
--- [title_description] 0-1165
Title: Reclassify benign WaitDelay outcome in script_legacy step

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Reclassify benign WaitDelay outcome in script_legacy step

The script_legacy step (used by `FF_SCRIPT_TO_STEP_MIGRATION`) returned `cmd.Run()` raw, so a backgrounded child that inherits the step's stdout pipe and outlives a cleanly-exited script trips gracefulexitcmd's `WaitDelay` and fails the job with `exec.ErrWaitDelay`.

Route the result through `normalizeExitError`, mirroring the concrete run path: `ErrWaitDelay` with exit code 0, and non-fatal user signals (`SIGUSR1`/ `USR2`/ `HUP`/ `PIPE`), are treated as success while real failures, `SIGKILL` and cancellation `SIGTERM` still propagate.

**NOTE**: Much of this code is just ported from Concrete. It will eventually be removed.

Temporary duplication here is probably a good thing as it allows us to test the mechanism under the smaller scoped `FF_SCRIPT_TO_STEP_MIGRATION`.

## Why was this MR needed?

See https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/gl-infra/production-engineering/-/work_items/29239

## What's the best way to test this MR?

There's a test included.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

--- [commits] 1166-1757
Commit messages:
Reclassify benign WaitDelay outcome in script_legacy step

The script_legacy step (used by FF_SCRIPT_TO_STEP_MIGRATION) returned
cmd.Run() raw, so a backgrounded child that inherits the step's stdout
pipe and outlives a cleanly-exited script trips gracefulexitcmd's
WaitDelay and fails the job with exec.ErrWaitDelay.

Route the result through normalizeExitError, mirroring the concrete run
path: ErrWaitDelay with exit code 0, and non-fatal user signals
(SIGUSR1/USR2/HUP/PIPE), are treated as success while real failures,
SIGKILL and cancellation SIGTERM still propagate.

--- [review_thread] 7778-8769
Review thread on shells/abstract.go:
@sxuereb: I'm not sure if we should turn this into an error and fail the build :thinking: Then again we simply don't support anything else so we *should assume* that refspec is present :thinking: ?
@tmaczukin: What will happen, if someone will use GitLab Runner 12.0+ with an older version of GitLab, that doesn't sent refspecs? Git fetch command will not have refspecs specified. This will fallback to default behavior just like `gitlab-runner exec` does.

For newer GitLab versions, where refspecs are being send, Runner will start working properly automatically.

I think we should be fine with dropping this warning and not failing the job in case, when the refspec info was not sent from GitLab.
@tmaczukin: @nolith May we get your opinion? You've authored the support for refspecs so you have probably the best knowledge of what edge cases can be and if our thinking is valid.
@nolith: It will run `git fetch --prune` in that case. It should be ok

=================== PR 4102   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4102
--- [title_description] 0-2460
Title: Make GitLab network client respect Retry-After header

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Implements a mechanism to wait during the number of seconds specified in the `Retry-After` header in a `HTTP 429 Too Many Requests` response from the server. It logs the error and then waits for the amount seconds specified in the `Retry-After` header. It can be graciously terminated by the user.

## Why was this MR needed?

In some situations (namely migrations involving the `ci_builds` table), it is important for [the server to be able to tell the runner to back off](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/395787) for a bit while it is undergoing maintenance. A mechanism for that is returning an `HTTP 429 Too Many Requests` response with a `Retry-After` value of for example 5 minutes. Currently, our implementation of for example artifacts uploader will [perform retries](https://go.dev/play/p/wunwfuAgBZk) starting [with 1 second and ending at 5 seconds](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/7bc4ce73dd49b4c6a6f18163343461539142ecf3/helpers/retry/retry.go#L13-14):

```
1s
2s
4s
5s
5s

Program exited.
```

This is clearly not enough for a maintenance of the `ci_builds` table, and has [resulted](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-com/gl-infra/production/-/issues/8521) in the need to revert migrations.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Open the GDK console with a recent GitLab repo and enable the FF:

    ```ruby
    ::Feature.enable(:runner_migrations_backoff)
    ```

1. Prepare a CI job before starting the runner
2. Start the runner on the GDK console, inside a lock so that the `HTTP 429` status code is returned to the runner:
    
    ```ruby
    Gitlab::Database::Migrations::RunnerBackoff::Communicator.new(String.class).execute_with_lock { sleep(120 [...]
--- [commits] 2461-2723
Commit messages:
Make GitLab network client respect Retry-After header

Allow the GitLab server to tell the runner to back off for a specified
period, for example during migrations.

Extract const

Write to log before sleeping

Allow cancelling of job

Add test

--- [review_thread] 9122-9706
Review thread on network/client.go:
@pedropombeiro: **note**:  the disadvantage of doing the `Sleep` call here is that the `429 Too Many Requests (Executing database migrations. Please retry later.)` log entry will only be created after the 1 minute wait. There are some alternatives:

1. Have the caller do the wait. This would require changing all callers of `doJSON`
2. Receive a logger function on `doJSON` which takes a result and logs the result. This would require changes to all the callers doing logging, along with the call chain.
@pedropombeiro: I've opted for approach 1.

--- [review_thread] 9707-10310
Review thread on network/gitlab.go:
@pedropombeiro: **note**:  I'm only checking for `Context` for the job polling requests, since that is the only path of the program that is currently prepared for it (and the most important to check).

Also, I didn't add tests for this, given that we'd need to test each API method to see if it waits for the specified time. Any suggestions on how to go about it? Should we test `handleResponse` and trust that all methods call it? Should we just test the job polling case, which is the most important/frequent?

UPDATE: I ended up just testing the job polling case.

=================== PR 1427   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1427
--- [title_description] 0-633
Title: Add STOPSIGNAL to gitlab-runner docker images

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Instructs docker to stop the gitlab-runner docker images using SIGQUIT instead of the default SIGTERM.

## Why was this MR needed?

On a busy system with many runners using the gitlab-runner image, it can be difficult to upgrade the image without
killing running builds.

With this change you can trigger an upgrade and know current builds will finish before the container quits and the
upgrade happens.

For cases where the container must die within a timeout, parameters such as "terminationGracePeriodSeconds" in Kubernetes can be used.

--- [commits] 634-792
Commit messages:
Add STOPSIGNAL to gitlab-runner docker images

By default SIGTERM is used which ungracefully kills all
running builds.  Use SIGQUIT instead.

=================== PR 5765   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5765
--- [title_description] 0-1258
Title: Parse the last line of stdout for UID/GID

Description:
## What does this MR do?

A Docker container can run commands on entrypoint that output to
stdout. For example, suppose you had an entrypoint script that does
this:

```
echo "Hello world"

exec "@$"
```

If `FF_DISABLE_UMASK_FOR_DOCKER_EXECUTOR` is enabled, this results in an error:

```
image UID: parsing "id -u" output: strconv.Atoi: parsing "Hello world\n1000": invalid syntax (stderr: )
```

Fix this by reading the last line of stdout.

This came out of investigation of a failed `dast` job that was ultimately fixed with https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/security-products/analyzers/browserker/-/merge_requests/1679.

### How to validate locally

1. I created this `entrypoint.sh`:

```shell
#!/bin/bash

echo "Hello world"

exec "$@"
```

2. Run `chmod +x entrypoint.sh`

3. Create this `Dockerfile`:

```dockerfile
FROM registry.gitlab.com/security-products/dast:6

COPY entrypoint.sh /browserker
```

4. `docker build . -t test:latest`

5. In my `runners.docker` config I set:

```toml
  [runners.docker]
    helper_image = "docker.io/library/gitlab-runner-helper:local"
    pull_policy = "if-not-present"
```

6. Rebuilt the helper and binary and configured a job to use `test:latest`.

Title: Add Docker integration tests for cache push for failed job

Description:
## What does this MR do?

- Add docker executor test for cache push on failed job
- Refactor docker tests job execution

## Why was this MR needed?

We introduced cache push for failed jobs in https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2350 however this was [failing for docker and Kubernetes executor](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2638). In https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2638 we fixed the problems however didn't add tests to make sure we don't break this behavior in the future.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Run test: `go test -v -run TestCacheInContainer ./executors/docker/`
1. Run jobs that fail and uploads cache to make sure the feature works as expected

    <details>
    <summary> .gitlab-ci.yml </summary>

    ```yml
    stages:
      - test
    
    cache-poc:
      stage: test
      image: alpine:latest
      cache:
        paths:
          - output/
        key: "$CI_COMMIT_REF_SLUG"
        untracked: true
        when: 'always'
        policy: pull-push
      script:
        - mkdir output || echo "Directory output already exists. Bypassing."
        - ls -l output
        - echo `date` >> output/log
        - cat output/log
        - exit 1
    
    ```
    </details>

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #27172

=================== PR 2609   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2609

Title: Gracefully fail unexpected Stream() calls

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Makes the random failures of `k8s integration` job (like the one tracked at https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/26716) to fail gracefuly. =================== PR 5918   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5918
--- [title_description] 0-1287
Title: Add namespace support to GitLab Secrets Manager

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Add namespace support to GitLab Secrets Manager

While secret path is correctly constructed to allow
`<namespace_path>/<engine_path>`, authentication occurs via a different
path, `<namespace_path>/auth/<auth_path>/<login_path>`; while commonly
`login_path` is just `"login"`, notably namespace and auth mount paths are
on different sides of the literal string `"auth/"`. This means the current
construction (only giving control over `<auth_path>`) does not work for
injecting namespaces from GitLab Secrets Manager into Runner's inline
authentication.

We resolve this by using a single fixed (full, minus the `/v1/` prefix)
authentication path, while retaining two-way compatibility with
older/newer GitLab Rails versions.

See also: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/554281

Signed-off-by: Alexander Scheel <ascheel@gitlab.com>

## Why was this MR needed?

See related thread: https://gitlab.slack.com/archives/C09GSEDT50U/p1760982437184459 

## What's the best way to test this MR?

TBD: opening a Rails change as well.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Follow-up to: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/554281

---

fyi: @ken.mcdonald @srajadas @iamricecake

--- [conversation] 8434-10038
Comment by @fcatteau:
Duo wrote:

> #### What This MR Changes
> 
> This MR adds **namespace support** to GitLab Secrets Manager authentication in GitLab Runner. It introduces a new `Path` field to the `GitLabSecretsManagerServerInlineAuth` struct that allows specifying the full authentication path, while maintaining backward compatibility with the existing `AuthMount` field.
> 
> **Key changes:**
> 
> 1. **New `Path` field** - Accepts the complete authentication path (e.g., `namespace/auth/jwt/login`)
> 2. **Backward compatibility** - Falls back to constructing the path from `AuthMount` if `Path` is not provided
> 3. **Updated logic** - The resolver now prefers the explicit `Path` over the templated `auth/<auth_mount>/login` construction
> 
> #### Why This Was Needed
> 
> The previous implementation had a **structural limitation** with namespace support. Here's the problem:
> 
> - **Secret paths** work correctly with namespaces: `<namespace_path>/<engine_path>`
> - **Authentication paths** have a different structure: `<namespace_path>/auth/<auth_path>/login`
> 
> The issue is that the namespace prefix comes **before** the literal `"auth/"` string, but the old `AuthMount` field only controlled what came **after** `"auth/"`. This made it impossible to inject namespaces into the authentication path.
> 
> **Example:**
> - Old way: Could only create `auth/jwt/login`
> - New way: Can create `my-namespace/auth/jwt/login`
> 
> This change enables GitLab Secrets Manager to work properly with OpenBao namespaces, which is critical for multi-tenant or isolated secret management scenarios.

=================== PR 5685   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/5685
--- [title_description] 0-3183
Title: Add support for 503 http code when the GitLab instance is in maintenance mode

Description:
## What does this MR do?

When GitLab enters maintenance mode, it returns a 503 Service Unavailable HTTP status code along with a Retry-After header. Upon receiving this response, GitLab Runner will begin polling GitLab at the interval specified in the Retry-After header.

## Why was this MR needed?

To prevent unnecessarily long waiting times once maintenance on the GitLab instance is completed.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

1. Use GDK with GitLab with the changes made in the branch: `503-in-maintenance-mode` (see MR: https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/merge_requests/195835)
2. Put GitLab in maintenance mode

$ gdk rails console

Review thread on network/client.go:
@ayufan: If we upload big trace, can we get to the point that 5 minute timeout will be not enoguh?
@ayufan: @tmaczukin ping
@tmaczukin: @ayufan A trace that will need more than 5 minutes to be uploaded will probably not be a common thing. But it's easy to imagine an artifact that is so big that it needs more time to be uploaded, and this timeout will affect also artifacts uploading.

The only reasonable solution I see is to add a `config.toml` setting that will define this timeout and set it to 10 or 15 minutes if the setting will be not defined. With such setting Runner's owner will be able to adjust request timeout like he can adjust for example trace length limit.

=================== PR 2704   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2704
--- [title_description] 0-1440
Title: Only allow failures with exit code 99 in Linux tests

Description:
## What does this MR do?

It leverages the new [`allow_failures:exit_codes`](https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/ci/yaml/#allow_failureexit_codes) functionality in GitLab Runner 13.8 in order to narrow down the types of failures that will be allowed.

We need to return a well-known exit code from the scripts that run tests (in this case 99) and only accept that error code (or success). Anything else (like a timeout) should cause the job to fail.

## Why was this MR needed?

Currently, a system failure like a timeout will be allowed (and therefore considered as a "good" build), which is not right.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Check if the Linux race unit tests still pass. I guess we'll need to keep an eye open to see if there are other error codes that will need to be allowed, but so far it doesn't seem so.

### Returning exit code 99 from tests :white_check_mark: 

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/jobs/1367722532 passes with an orange badge

### Returning exit code 222 from tests :white_check_mark: 

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/jobs/1367741810 fails with a red badge

### Returning exit code 99 from tests, only accepting exit code 99, timeout expired :white_check_mark: 

https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/jobs/1367741818 fails with a red badge

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Part of #27474

Title: Fix kubernetes executor helper image override log

Fixes an incorrect debug log statement in the Kubernetes executor.

When using the Kubernetes executor with a custom `helper_image` value, said image is used (we know this from the underlying container runtime's logs), but debug logs say it's using the default helper image.

The issue is fixed by logging `helper_image` instead if its in-config value is `!= ""`, which is the same mechanism that the Docker executor has [here](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/executors/docker/docker.go?ref_type=heads#L269-279).

This isn't pretty in any of both cases (Docker and Kubernetes), but in the Kubernetes executor's case, I checked why we can't use the image value used in the actual Pod definition and we can't because said Pod definition is never passed to [`Prepare(...)`](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/executors/kubernetes/kubernetes.go#L340-419), which is the method that [logs this line](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/executors/kubernetes/kubernetes.go#L394). The pod definition dies at [`setupBuildPod(...)`](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/main/executors/kubernetes/kubernetes.go#L1953-2002), where it is no longer passed down the call stack.
So, we either log there (ugly); or we keep logging in `Prepare(...)` using the va [...]

=================== PR 1990   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1990
--- [title_description] 0-776
Title: Start watching container before starting

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Start watching container before starting

## Why was this MR needed?

When the machine is resource constrain the go routine to check the
status of the container might not be scheduled soon enough and the
container might be removed, which would result into a 404 when searching
for the container.

Make sure that `waitForContainer` has started before the container is
started.

## Are there points in the code the reviewer needs to double check?

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [x] Documentation created/updated
- [x] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [x] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

reference #4450

--- [commits] 777-1226
Commit messages:
Start watching container before starting

When the machine is resource constrain the go routine to check the
status of the container might not be scheduled soon enough and the
container might be removed, which would result into a 404 when searching
for the container.

Make sure that `waitForContainer` has started before the container is
started.

reference https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/issues/4450#note_316278797

--- [review_thread] 4668-4788
Review thread on executors/docker/docker.go:
@pedropombeiro: Would it make sense to call this `containerStatusCreated`?

--- [review_thread] 4789-6577
Review thread on executors/docker/docker.go:
@pedropombeiro: I don't think it makes sense to pass `started` to `waitForContainer` just so it can close it. This seems to be a caller implementation detail. This would avoid having callers create a channel that isn't going to get used (like in `volumesManagerAdapter.WaitForContainer`).
@sxuereb: The idea behind this `started` channel is so that we can signal the user that we actually started watching the container, so it can continue with whatever it wants to do (for example starting the container) when we know that the "watch" actually started. This follows a commonly used pattern, as explained a bit in https://golang.org/doc/effective_go.html#channels using the `quit` channel, which is the same idea.

Do you think we should not signal the caller that we actually started watching the container :thinking: How can we make sure that we are safe to start the container if we can't be sure that the watcher started :thinking: ? Would adding a comment make it more clear?
@pedropombeiro: What I meant is doing the following instead:

```go
	waitStarted := make(chan struct{})
	waitCh := make(chan error, 1)
	go func() {
                close(waitStarted)
		waitCh <- e.waitForContainer(e.Context, id)
	}()
```

There's no real difference between calling `close(waitStarted)` just before calling `waitForContainer` (as long as it is done inside the go routine), and this means you no longer need to create a channel in situations where you're not using go routines (e.g. `terminalConn.Start` or `volumesManagerAdapter.WaitForContainer`).
@sxuereb: Ah! That is much cleaner for sure! Sorry I didn't understand your original message I thought we shouldn't use the `started` channel at all. Update, and thank you for the suggestion :bow:

--- [review_thread] 6578-7787
Review thread on executors/docker/docker.go:
@pedropombeiro: I see other existing `channel` variables with a `*Ch` suffix (e.g. `waitCh`). We should be consistent in the naming.
@sxuereb: I'm not a big fan of attaching `Ch` when there is already clear name. To mee it sounds like `containerNameString` which doesn't make sense in a strongly typed language. This is explained a bit in detail in https://dave.cheney.net/2019/01/29/you-shouldnt-name-your-variables-after-their-types-for-the-same-reason-you-wouldnt-name-your-pets-dog-or-cat. We also have a mixture of this in the codebase, for example [runFinished](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/d174dc487a1c7b3db77dcc1631e20bf9610294f0/commands/multi.go#L86), and [buildFinished](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/master/common/build.go#L430) WDYT?
@pedropombeiro: I'm fine either way, just noticed that we currently have two ways of naming the same type of variables, which I found odd. Channels are kind of special to me, since looking at the `waitStarted` name without seeing the declaration, I would assume it's a `bool`. But I guess with today's editors it's not such a big deal and the type is always easily visible.

Review thread on executors/docker/docker.go:
@tmaczukin: @steveazz 

```suggestion:-0+0
func containerCreated(ctr types.ContainerJSON) bool {
```

A _Running_ container is also a _Created_ one. But a _Created_ container doesn't need to be _Running_ :wink:
@sxuereb: @tmaczukin exactly, we are also checking that the container is [running](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/blob/36006770c32ca87ad72e2e133ba455b3b402edb6/executors/docker/docker.go#L880) :thinking:
@tmaczukin: We're checking if it's running **or** created, because created is enough for the `waitForContainer` to be used.

So finally, we're interested in `isContainerCreated()` not `isContainerRunning()`.
@tmaczukin: In other words - we're not interested if the container is running or not. But since _Running_ wraps _Created_ (and there is only one state at the time) we must check if this is any of them. But the purpose is to know that the container was created.

Forgot a moment about the function implementation and look on the name it's using now - `containerRunning()`. And let's consider a case when we want to add some behavior, that can be done only on a running container.

The developer who will be adding it, will see the `containerRunning() bool` function and will think "So I can use it to check if the container is running or not!". And will use it. And then we will end with an error, because at the usage time, in a specific case, this returned `true` while the container wasn't really running. It was just created and not started yet.

--- [conversation] 11481-11531
Comment by @sxuereb:
Back to you @tmaczukin :bow:

--- [conversation] 11532-13217
Comment by @tmaczukin:
We've reverted this MR with !2026.

Changing the ordering of when we start the container and when we start waiting for it to exit (and to check the exit code) was making no difference for the `build_script` step execution, because the container was either in `Created` or `Running` state.

It creates however a problem for the `after_script` step, because Runner re-uses the main job container to execute there also the `after_script` script. And here the situation is different: the container is already in `Exited` state so the call to `waitForContainer()` exits immediately reporting the "status" of the previous step (so either a failed script or no failure). With this, after Runner starts the container again and sends the `after_script` to it's STDIN, the final `select {}` in the `watchContainer()` returns quickly on the `err = <-waitCh` case, which in reality means, that the script is not executed at all (or only the beginning of it is executed, but there is no time to even gather the initial output).

We've decided that there is a very low possibility of falling into the specific case that this MR tried to handle. While the change introduced here created a nasty ~regression - https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/215037 - which made `after_script` not executable, even when defined.

Therefore we've decided to revert this MR. We think that the other MRs that we've merged in %12.10 and that were addressing the two old, nasty bugs in Docker executor, should be enough to make the initial problems solved. If not and if we will get any new reports similar to #4450, we will revisit this MR, but having the `after_script` case in mind.

Title: Revert "Merge branch 'avonbertoldi/git-lfs-is-bad' into 'main'"

```
Undo!

=================== PR 2573   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/2573
--- [title_description] 0-3667
Title: Use helper image to change K8s log dir permissions

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Use helper image to change K8s log dir permissions

## Why was this MR needed?

Background:
With the new [Docker Hub
limits](https://docs.docker.com/docker-hub/download-rate-limit/) we
shouldn't be using `busybox` image since it would mean that users will
get rate limited when running jobs.

Solution:
Use the helper image to change the permissions. The helper image is
still hosted in Docker Hub, however in
#27196 we are moving
the helper image to `registry.gitlab.com` behind a feature flag
`FF_GITLAB_REGISTRY_HELPER_IMAGE`.

Notes:
This solves the problem of users in airgapped environments, because
they can [override the helper
images](https://docs.gitlab.com/runner/configuration/advanced-configuration.html#overriding-the-helper-image).

There is also a big size difference on images. The `busybox` image is
only around 1.2MB whilst the helper image is around 65MB. The container
is a lot larger, but this brings consistency on the Kubernetes execution
where we only use the user image and our helper image, which is going to
be decoupled from Docker Hub. We can have a `busybox` image hosted inside
of `registry.gitlab.com` but it might be premature optimization right now.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

### Make sure jobs with nonroot images still run

1. Use the following `.gitlab-ci.yml` for the Kubernetes executor

    <details>
    <summary> .gitlab-ci.yml </summary>

    ```yaml
    variables:
      SLEEP: 0
      FF_USE_LEGACY_KUBERNETES_EXECUTION_STRATEGY: "false"

    stages:
    - root
    - alpine
    - debian

    root:
      image: alpine
      stage: root
      before_script:
      - id
      script:
      - mkdir -p test-root
      - echo "test" > test-roo [...]

--- [conversation] 3929-4088
Comment by @tmaczukin:
@nolith Could you approve and merge this? It's quite important since our unit tests suite is not reliable at this moment :disappointed:

--- [conversation] 4089-4234
Comment by @nolith:
@tmaczukin at first glance I cannot see why this diff solves the problem.

--- [conversation] 4235-6763
Comment by @tmaczukin:
@nolith Sure.

The fix itself was done by f517e3cd718995420a67690cf7188ff9ec2a5614. Current problem was caused because `exitCode` was not set as `local` and was used in two different functions where one function called another one. In short it looked like:

```bash
set -eo pipeline

test() {
  // prepare test
  exitCode=0
  go test ${1} || exitCode=1

  return ${exitCode}
}

tests() {
  exitCode=0
  for package in ${packages}; do
    test ${package} || exitCode=1
  done

  exit ${exitCode}
}

tests package1 package2
```

To make it clear: the `|| exitCode=1` pattern is used in the `test()` function, because with `set -e` go test failure would break the command execution. And we want to execute tests for all packages and eventually fail the script but only if there was any `go test` failure caught.

The problem is that bash's variables are global by default. So how execution of the above looks like:

1. We're setting `exitCode` in `tests()` to `0` and start the loop
1. We're executing test for the first package.
1. The `test()` internally re-uses `exitCode` variable and set's it to `0` for it own purpose. Nothing bad happens.
1. Test fails, `exitCode` in `test()` is set to `1` and it's returned as function's exit code.
1. `exitCode` in `tests()` is set to 1 as expected. Still nothing bad happens.
1. We're executing test for the second package.
1. The `test()` internally re-uses `exitCode` variable and set's it to `0` for it own purpose. **Since `exitCode` in this case is global, we're overwriting the value previously saved in `tests()`. This is the bug here**.
1. Test passes, `exitCode` is left unchanged and it's returned as function's exit code.
1. `exitCode` in `tests()` is left unchanged, but after it was changed by `test()` internals. So it was [...]
--- [conversation] 6764-6934
Comment by @nolith:
Thanks @tmaczukin 

I got it was related to `exitCode` being global, but I missed the loop and wasn't clear to me where that variable was reset to 0.

Title: Disable interactive git credentials

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Disables interactive git credentials. This appears to be important for Windows, where it looks like it doesn't always detect that credential input cannot be provided.

## Why was this MR needed?

CI/CD jobs are confusingly stuck if git asks for credentials interactively.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

Manual test on Windows with gitlab.com/ajwalker/runner-backbench with the job:

```
build should fail and not ask interactively ask for credentials:
  job:
    runner_info:
      timeout: 10

    git_info:
      repo_url: https://gitlab.com/jobd/jobd # some private repo

    steps:
      - name: script
        script: [echo Hello World]
  exit_code: 128
  failure_reason: script_failure
```

On Windows, this works with a clear message in the job log: `Cannot prompt because user interactivity has been disabled.`.

On MacOS, the username/password UI prompt still appears. So this is a good direction, but still doesn't solve the problem everywhere. This feels like a bug on MacOS.

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

Closes #4134

=================== PR 1174   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/1174
--- [title_description] 0-1471
Title: Split `make docker` for GitLab Runner Helper

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Split `make docker` for GitLab Runner Helper to build binaries and containers seperatrly

## Why was this MR needed?

GitLab Runner helper is built using `make docker` which builds the
binary, creates a Docker container and extract the files system of that
container for publishing at a later stage.

Because of  #3755 we
have to split this command up so we generate the binaries and the docker
archive separately since Windows containers cannot be built on Linux systems
or vice versa.

We are keeping the binary generation in one command to have both
Windows and Linux binaries consistent, and the diverging on how we
create the containers since they need to done separately.

The create of the container for Linux based image will stay in the
Makefile since it's perfectly fine. For windows it will be separate
powershell script since to run natively on Windows since we cannot run
bash on windows server.

The reason for this, so that we keep the binary
generation consistent between both platforms and then

Drop support for `USE_PRECOMPILED_IMAGES` since it's just extra
complexity that is not needed.

## Does this MR meet the acceptance criteria?

- [ ] Documentation created/updated
- [x] Added tests for this feature/bug
- [x] In case of conflicts with `master` - branch was rebased

## What are the relevant issue numbers?

#3755

--- [commits] 1472-2769
Commit messages:
Split `make docker` for GitLab Runner Helper

GitLab Runner helper is built using `make docker` which builds the
binary, creates a Docker container and extract the files system of that
container for publishing at a later stage.

Because of  https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/3755 we
have to split this command up so we generate the binaries and the docker
archive separately since Windows containers cannot be built on Linux systems
or vice versa.

We are keeping the binary generation in one command to have both Windows
and Linux binaries consistent, and built them on a Linux platform where
`make` is installed. The make command already sets up the $GOPATH,
dependencies and environment for building the binaries so there is no
point of duplicating that. Then diverge when building the docker
containers, since they have to be done on different platforms.  Windows
does have GNUMake http://gnuwin32.sourceforge.net/packages/make.htm but
it's fairly outdated and doesn't seem to work when installing it on
Windows server 2019.

Drop support for `USE_PRECOMPILED_IMAGES` since it's just extra
complexity that is not needed, and it doesn't seem to be used anywhere.
If the user runs `make helper-docker` and the binaries are already
present they are not rebuild.

--- [review_thread] 9033-9898
Review thread on Makefile.runner_helper.mk:
@nolith: what are those `windows1803` and `windows1809`? Is this due to `gox`?
@sxuereb: Since we need to differentiate between the windows images for the [Dockerfiles](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/merge_requests/1167) because we [need to have matching windows versions](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/issues/3755#note_135627628) when building a Windows container, I though it was a good idea to follow the same "pattern" for the binaries, but rethinking it, it doesn't make much sense we are rebuilding the same thing and wasting compute time. Using a single binary is simpler, we would just have to update out Dockerfiles to the same binary, I've updated this MR and https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/merge_requests/1167 to reflect the change.
@nolith: thanks for the explaination

=================== PR 4064   https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab-runner/-/merge_requests/4064
--- [title_description] 0-2114
Title: Unresolved secrets now return error by default

Description:
## What does this MR do?

Unresolved secrets now return an error by default.

For now, this behaviour can be disabled by setting the Runner feature flag `FF_SECRET_RESOLVING_FAILS_IF_MISSING` to false.

## Why was this MR needed?

Unresolved secrets were returning "<nil>", making it hard to determine _why_ a build wasn't working as expected.

## What's the best way to test this MR?

- You need a GitLab Ultimate (or a test GitLab instance). If using gitlab.com, you can request this here: https://about.gitlab.com/handbook/incentives/#gitlab-ultimate
- You need a Vault installation. You may want to create a Hashicorp Vault cloud account (https://portal.cloud.hashicorp.com/sign-up) and create a development cluster.
- Install vault cli: `brew install vault-cli`
- `vault-cli login` to login.
- Create JWT auth config:
  ```
  vault write auth/jwt/config jwks_url="https://gitlab.com/-/jwks" bound_issuer="gitlab.com"
  ```
- Create policy:
  ```
  echo 'path "ops/data/production/*" { capabilities = [ "read" ] }' | vault policy write a-policy -
  ```
- Create JWT role:
  ```
  echo '{"role_type": "jwt", "policies": ["a-policy"], "user_claim": "user_login", "bound_claims_type": "glob", "bound_claims": {"ref":"main"}}' | vault write auth/jwt/role/my-role -
  ```
- Update auth config with default role:
  ```
  vault write auth/jwt/config jwks_url="https://gitlab.com/-/jwks" bound_issuer="gitlab.com" role=my-role
  ```
- Configure a job with the vault server url: `VAULT_SERVER_URL` = `https://vault-cluster-public-vault-....hashicorp.cloud:8200`
- Configure job to fetch some secret from vault:
  ```
  a-job:
    id_tokens:
      VAULT_ID_TOKEN:
        aud: https://gitlab.com
    secrets:
      DATABASE_PASSWORD:
     [...]
